# Preface to this First Part.

->Beloved Reader in GOD and His eternal Truth!<-

It is now a quite general saying and lament among all people that the times and also the people nowadays are very evil, which in truth is indeed the case; and if I am to reveal my thoughts on such matters quite sincerely <note>original: "ungeheuchelt"; meaning without hypocrisy or pretense. Schütz is signaling the Radical Pietist value of "heart-honesty."</note>, I do not believe that a worse and more wicked generation of men has ever lived in the world than this present generation. And whoever has received even a little enlightenment from GOD, and has learned what a righteous character is like in Christ <note>original: "Christo"; the author uses the Latin dative form of Christ's name.</note>, will very gladly believe me when I say that we are living right now in the dreadful times which the Holy Spirit prophesied and pointed to long ago—especially through his chosen vessel <note>original: "Rüstzeuge"; literally "tool" or "equipment," a common biblical metaphor (Acts 9:15) for a person used by God.</note>, Paul <note>original: "Paulum"; the Latin accusative form of Paul.</note>—stating that they would come in the last days. This is because the distressing evidence, alas! testifies more than enough to everyone; namely, that all cities and lands are quite full of such people as the aforementioned dear Apostle described in his second <note>Schütz is referring here to the "Second" Epistle to Timothy, specifically chapter 3, which describes the perils of the last days.</note>

<meta>catchword: Letter</meta>

<summary>Schütz opens his preface with a bleak assessment of the 18th-century moral landscape, claiming that the current generation is the most wicked in history. He frames these "evil times" as the fulfillment of the Apostle Paul's prophecies concerning the end of days.</summary>
<keywords>Preface, last days, moral decay, Apostle Paul, enlightenment, wickedness, Radical Pietism</keywords>

--- Page 9 ---
<meta>This page continues the Preface. The previous page ended with a reference to the Apostle Paul's prophecy about the "last days" in his second epistle.</meta>

) 5 (
A 3

Letter to Timothy, Ch. 3 / 1 to 9 <note>original: "Brief an Timotheum"; Schütz is citing 2 Timothy 3:1-9, which describes people being "lovers of themselves" and "having a form of godliness but denying its power."</note>. As such is known to all the world, and no truth-loving person will deny.

And although, according to the saying of the Psalmist <note>original: "Psalmisten"; a reference to Psalm 116:11, "I said in my haste, All men are liars."</note>, all men are liars, and also in this world, among this present wicked generation, nothing is more hated than the truth: yet they do speak the truth in such general saying and lamenting; but this alone is to be lamented in them: that they do not believe in their hearts this distressing matter which they confess and lament with their mouths, and thus, according to their own thoughts, they speak it only as an impudent lie which they themselves do not hold to be true at all—and would likely never speak if they believed it were true. This is because they are altogether accustomed to lies and are enemies of the truth from the heart. 

And to whomsoever this speech of mine regarding such impudent liars <note>original: "Lügen-Redner"; literally "lie-speakers" or those who practice falsehood.</note> might seem strange, I ask that he would closely consider and further reflect upon that which I shall now briefly report for the confirmation <note>original: "Bekräfftigung"; the act of strengthening or proving an argument.</note> of this matter; then he will clearly find that it is exactly so with them as I have reported here, and that they altogether believe nothing less,

<meta>catchword: than (als)</meta>

<summary>Schütz argues that although contemporary people complain about the wickedness of the age, they are hypocritical because they do not truly believe the gravity of their own complaints. He asserts that the public lamentation of "evil times" is often just a hollow habit of a generation that has become naturally hostile to the truth.</summary>

<keywords>hypocrisy, truth vs. lies, Second Timothy, Psalm 116, last days, spiritual blindness, corruption of the heart</keywords>

than that the times and people are evil, regardless of the fact that they are always speaking of it and complaining about it.

For when ten or even a hundred people of every <term>status</term><note>original: "Standes"; referring to one's social rank or estate in the early modern period.</note>, gender, and age are gathered together, and they all complain together about the evil times and people; and if there is perhaps a pious and truth-loving person among them who says to them: "Yes, this is just as true as you say; and you yourselves are also altogether included in the number of such evil people, and must therefore necessarily become better, convert to **GOD** in truth, and begin a holy life, if you wish to come to **GOD** and live with Him eternally and be <term>blessed</term><note>original: "seelig"; meaning both spiritually happy and eternally saved.</note>, etc." Then they immediately begin to excuse themselves one after another, and present themselves as nothing but pious and honest people who have no need for any improvement and are already more than good enough. 

And if one then steps a bit closer to them and points out their gross vices and failings as if with a finger, so that they cannot deny them, they then appeal to their human weakness and sinful birth, and say that they are poor sinners and weak humans, and if

to light / and to strengthen them with <term>scriptural passages</term><note>Original: "Schrifft-Sprüche." These are specific verses from the Bible used as legal or theological proof-texts.</note>.

Because I did not wish to write a completely new and extensive <term>treatise</term><note>A formal, systematic written discourse on a specific subject.</note> on this subject for this occasion / I have taken my <term>song</term><note>The author refers to a hymn or poem he composed to summarize his theology; music and verse were common tools for teaching in this period.</note> / which I previously sang regarding this matter / and which is printed and can be read in my *The Voice of the Bridegroom and the Bride* / pages 26 to 32 / and laid it as the foundation of this little work / and have explained the same from section to section / and strengthened it with evidence from the Holy Scripture.

Since, however, everyone likely has the Bible in their house / I have only noted those passages of the Holy Scripture / which clearly testify to what I have put forward in my song and its explanation / and so I ask / that the beloved reader would not begrudge the effort / and would look up and read all such passages themselves / for then he will find sufficient ground. I would have liked to have had all such scriptural passages / or at least the most significant and clearest ones / printed in the work / but because the little work would have become twice as large in that way / and also since I do not have the <term>publishing funds</term><note>Original: "Verlags-Mittel." This provides a glimpse into the material reality of 17th or 18th-century printing, where authors often had to self-fund their publications.</note> to spare / I have therefore done it in this manner.

It is indeed with me in this regard / as also with the second and third parts of this little work

<meta>This page continues the sentence from page 10, completing the word "little work" (Wercklein) and explaining that the author’s motivation is spiritual rather than intellectual or coercive.</meta>

...little work is not done because I wish to bring something new to the ears of the <term>curious world</term><note>In this context, "curious" refers to those who seek novelty for its own sake rather than for spiritual improvement.</note> / or even want to force anyone to believe these things / but solely for the glory of God and the conversion of mankind; as will be mentioned again in the preface to the second part. And certainly / even if someone already believed all this that I have written here and held it as true / but nevertheless remained ungodly / then I would not take the slightest pleasure in him; however, if someone is <term>pious from the heart</term><note>A hallmark of Pietist thought, which prioritized personal, heartfelt devotion over the mere intellectual "holding-for-true" of church dogmas.</note> / then he is dear and pleasant to me from the heart / even if he did not believe such things as the <term>T. J. R.</term><note>Abbreviation for *Tausendjähriges Reich*—the "Thousand-Year Reign" or Millennium, a period of Christ's rule on earth mentioned in Revelation 20.</note> and the <term>W. U. D.</term><note>Likely stands for *Wiederbringung aller Dinge*—the "Restoration of all things" or universal salvation, a controversial belief that eventually everyone, including the damned, would be reconciled to God.</note>; because I know / that the knowledge and holding-for-true of these things / saves no one / but rather faith in God and the keeping of His word.

Regarding the main title of this little work / I suspect indeed / that it will not please everyone / and will provide my <term>detractors</term><note>Original: "Neidern," literally "enviers." The author anticipates that his theological claims will be met with mockery or jealousy from orthodox critics.</note> with a new cause for mockery; however, I inform the truth-loving reader / that I have given and assigned such a beautiful title to this little work not without good reason / for therein is not only a clear testimony of the future golden and lovely-

He also anticipates criticism regarding the bold title he has chosen for the book.</summary>

blossoming time / which also the **Prophet Micah** with express words calls a **Golden Rose** / chapter 4 / verse 8. <note>original: "güldene Rose." In some mystical traditions and older German interpretations of Micah 4:8, the "Tower of the Flock" (Migdal-Eder) was symbolically associated with a "Golden Rose" representing the restored glory of Jerusalem.</note> But it also contains a further testimony of the noble power and virtue of **Jesus Christ** / as the most beautiful <term>Flower of Sharon</term><note>A reference to the "Rose of Sharon" from the Song of Solomon 2:1, a common metaphor in Pietist literature for the beauty and spiritual fragrance of Christ.</note> and **Rose in the Valley** / Song of Solomon 2 / 1. It describes how this same power—namely in its greening and lovely-blooming strength and noble scent—throughout all eternity of eternities, will reveal and communicate itself to all humbled and broken spirits / and refresh them / and make them green and blooming in the new life of the Spirit / etc.; 

Thus I have indeed been able, with good reason and right, to call it a **Golden Rose** / or rather a testimony and humble depiction of a **Golden Rose**. Yet this is not the only reason alone / which moved me to give this <term>little work</term><note>original: "Wercklein." A humble diminutive often used by spiritual authors to describe their own writings.</note> such a title; rather, there is also this additional cause / that / because once one of our <term>preachers</term><note>Likely an Orthodox Lutheran clergyman who opposed the author's Pietist or mystical leanings.</note> said to my face / as if my previous printed little works / had given off a "foul stench" / (whereby he perhaps alluded to the foul stench and filth of the great lies and blasphemies / which the wicked enemy has spat out at me because of this matter) and thus quite perver-

<summary>The author justifies his book's title "The Golden Rose" by citing biblical metaphors for Christ's beauty and reveals he chose it partly as a response to a critic who insulted his previous writings as having a "foul stench."</summary>

--- Page 17 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's defense of his book's title, "The Golden Rose." He responds to a specific critic—likely an orthodox cleric mentioned on the previous page—who accused his writings of having a "foul stench."</meta>

in a perverse way ascribes such a stench to me and my **little work**, even though it is quite obvious that **Satan** has brought it forth from his abyss through the foul stink-holes of his lying and blaspheming mouths, and has spewed it out upon me and my little books; as more will be reported concerning this in the **preface** to this second part. Therefore, with this title right on the first page of this <term>little work</term><note>original: "Wercklein." A term of humility used by the author to describe his own writing.</note>, in defiance of such **enviers** and to the honor of my **God**, I have wished to show that these wholesome teachings and precious **truths**, which I have presented in my little book, are no foul stench, but are rather to be compared to a **golden**, or beautiful and sweet-smelling **rose**. 

And even though these little works of mine are but a meager testimony and a clumsy painting of such a **golden rose**, and thus are not the rose itself, I yet hope that the beautiful <term>Flower of Sharon</term> and lovely **Rose of the Valley**, **Jesus Christ**, will accompany these my simple little works and testimonies of Him with His sweet scent and noble power, and will refresh all of <term>God’s little bees</term><note>A popular 17th-century metaphor for pious readers who, like bees gathering nectar, extract spiritual nourishment from a text.</note>, who gather there with a pure **desire for honey**. But to the poisonous **spiders**, who are only accustomed to searching for poison, <note>The author contrasts the "bee" (the humble, faithful reader) with the "spider" (the malicious critic who twists the meaning of the text to find "poison").</note>

<summary>The author defends his work against critics who labeled it a "foul stench," arguing instead that his teachings reflect the spiritual fragrance of Jesus Christ, the "Golden Rose." He contrasts the "godly bees" who find sweetness in his words with "poisonous spiders" who seek only to criticize.</summary>
<keywords>Golden Rose, Jesus Christ, Flower of Sharon, spiritual fragrance, God's little bees, religious controversy, humility, Satan, Pietism</keywords>

--- Page 18 ---
<meta>This page concludes the author's preface and defense of his work. It continues the metaphor from the previous page regarding how different readers approach a text—specifically comparing the "bees" (the faithful) to the "spiders" (the critics).</meta>

and even when they suck from the most noble flowers, such sweet fragrance and noble power will remain well hidden from them. Because they seek only poison—that is, only something to criticize and mock—they will surely find it, to their own great harm and ruin. But the noble power and virtue of **Jesus Christ** will remain hidden from them. <note>The author concludes his metaphor: just as a spider extracts poison from the same flower where a bee finds honey, a malicious critic will find only "poison" in a holy text.</note>

With this, I heartily commend the truth-loving reader to the healing grace of **God**, and remain one who wishes for the salvation of all people and seeks only to please **God**.

Written in the days when all the high heads of **Europe** were arming themselves for a bloody war, and the author of this <term>little work</term><note>original: "Wercklein." A diminutive used by the author as a gesture of humility regarding his writing.</note> joins himself with the **King of all kings** and **Lord of all lords** in a strict <term>defensive and offensive alliance</term><note>original: "Defensiv- und Offensiv-Allianz." The author uses the political and military language of his time to describe his spiritual commitment to God.</note> against all his enemies and the enemies of His kingdom, making himself ready and prepared to fight, to shun, to suffer, and to endure.

->C. Schütz.<-

<summary>The author finishes his metaphorical defense against critics and concludes the preface by dedicating himself to God's service amidst the backdrop of European war. He signs the work with a declaration of spiritual alliance with Christ.</summary>

# A Song of the Thousand-Year Glorious Kingdom of Jesus Christ.

->To the melody / of the same beginning / or:<-
->Why should I then grieve.<-
<note>The author suggests setting these lyrics to the tune of "Warum sollt ich mich denn grämen," a well-known Lutheran hymn composed by Paul Gerhardt in 1653.</note>

---

->1.<-
Joyfully my heart shall leap; because the time full of joy now presses in, when **Jehovah** <note>The Hebrew name for God, often used in the 17th and 18th centuries to emphasize His eternal sovereignty.</note> alone shall be high and shall be honored upon the earth.

->2.<-
Yes / the **Lord** shall become glorious at that time / far and wide / over the whole earth. The knowledge of Him shall cover every land. He shall stretch out His hand far.

->3.<-
All people shall recognize **God**; and gladly call Him their **Lord** / **God** and **Savior**. No one will teach another, because they / late and early / shall hear **God** Himself.

->4.<-
All people shall be righteous / and gladly serve the **Lord** as faithful servants. The sinners of a hundred years, completely godless / are cursed / by the **Lord's** hosts. <note>This refers to the prophecy in Isaiah 65:20, implying that in this era, a life of a hundred years is but a short span, and only those under a curse would perish so "young."</note>

->5.<-
Then **God** will establish His kingdom; and at the same time overthrow and destroy **Satan's** kingdom. He will punish many nations; and sweep away the army that is so very evil.

<column-break/>

->6.<-
Then the seed of **Israel** / all of them / whose number is known to **God** by name; shall be converted to the **Lord** and at the same time be led back into their kingdom.

->7.<-

->10.<-

[To] explain the others <note>This likely refers to the "other" points or stanzas of the hymn previously mentioned.</note> briefly and fortify them with Scriptural passages: And I ask that the beloved reader would read all of this with an impartial mind and good consideration, and also look up and read all the cited Scripture passages; and when he does so, he will then very soon be fully convinced of the truth of this doctrine, and afterwards know how to apply it for **God’s** glory and his soul’s salvation and eternal well-being.

So know then now, my beloved friends and sisters, <note>The original German "Freunde und Freundinnen" specifically addresses both men and women, a common practice in Radical Pietist circles that emphasized the spiritual equality of the sexes.</note> that **God** the **Lord** in the beginning created the first man, Adam, according to His image, and appointed him as a king and ruler in this outer worldly kingdom; that he should rule therein over the birds under the sky, over the fish in the sea and waters, and over all creatures <term>Gethierte: an archaic term for animals or living things</term> upon the earth; and the noblest and best fruits and products of the soil, which the earth produced in abundance without any toil or labor, he should enjoy for his food and delight; Genesis 1:26–29. 

And for all of this, the benevolent **God** then required nothing further from man than that he should recognize Him as his **God** and Over-**Lord** and sole benefactor, and also, as such, fear, love, and honor Him; and to yield his whole heart, his love, and his life to Him in the greatest gratitude for all such great benefits, and constantly offer them up in pure reverence and childlike obedience of love. Deuteronomy 6; Proverbs 23:26. And then all the world will rest and be still, and joyfully shout when the **oars** <note>original: *Ruder*; likely a metaphorical reference to the power or "steering" of the wicked, though it may also be a variation of *Ruten* (rods or scepters)</note> of the godless are broken, and the **driver** himself—who struck the nations in his wrath—lies in the **fiery pool** <note>original: *feuriger Pful*; the "Lake of Fire" described in the Book of Revelation</note>.

For **Christ the Lord** shall, on such a **Day of Judgment**, seize the two above-mentioned **incarnate devils**—namely the lamb-horned **Priest-Beast** <term>Pfaffen-Thier; a derogatory term for corrupt clergy, specifically the "False Prophet" of Revelation</term> and the ten-horned **Regent-Beast** <term>Regenten-Thier; representing worldly political tyranny, specifically the "Beast from the Sea"</term>—alive and throw them into the **fiery pool**. At that time, all the **stinking goats** <note>A biblical metaphor for the damned or the hypocritical, based on the separation of sheep and goats in Matthew 25</note> of the world, and cruel **tyrants** and godless **idol-priests** who have ever lived on the earth, shall be awakened and thrown with them into the **fiery pool**. 

And so, as a "privilege" <note>The author uses *Vorrecht* (privilege/right) ironically here to mean a first-priority punishment</note> for their evil works and deeds, they shall spend these **thousand years** <note>The Millennium, a thousand-year period described in Revelation 20 during which Christ reigns</note> in such a place of **torment** with both body and soul. Meanwhile, the other dead—who were not quite so utterly godless, but who nonetheless have not yet fully received their deserved evil reward by the final **Day of Judgment**—shall only arrive there after the thousand years; until that time, they remain in other **prisons** and dwelling-places where the **torment** is not quite as great as in the pool. 

In the same way, the **Saints** and the **Elect** shall live and reign with **Christ** in His joys during this time as a privilege and a special reward of grace for their holiness, virtue, and good works. This continues until the others—who were indeed pious, but not yet entirely holy and godly, having still possessed many **dross** <note>original: *Schlacken*; impurities or residues of sin</note> and vices—shall finally join them after the thousand years have passed.

### The Third Little Law. <note>original: "Gesetzlein." A diminutive form of "law," used here to describe a short, poetic rule of faith or a theological principle.</note>

All people will know God / and gladly call him their Lord God and Savior. No one will teach another / because they will hear God himself both late and early.

### Explanation and Proof.

Because in the preceding little law it was shown, among other things, that at such a time the land would be full of the <term>knowledge of the Lord; an experiential, intimate understanding of God rather than just academic facts</term>, according to Isaiah 11:9; so this doctrinal statement is now repeated and explained once more at the beginning of this little law / and it is indicated there / that one should not understand this simply as the physical land in which people live—but rather as the people who live in the land; namely, that all the people who live in the land / or on the earth during such a <term>golden time; the anticipated thousand-year reign of peace or the "Millennium"</term> / would collectively know God; as it says at the beginning of this little verse: All people will know God, etc. For this manner of speaking is quite common to the Holy Spirit, that he addresses or names the land / or even heaven and earth / when he wants to address the people. As this is well known to all who understand the Scriptures / and can also be seen in Deuteronomy 32:1, Isaiah 1:2, Jeremiah 6:19, Ezekiel 6:2, 3, 4, Hosea 1, 2, 3, etc.

Furthermore, it is taught in this little law / that at such a time among the entire people of God / no one would teach another nor exhort them to the knowledge of the Lord / but they would all know him very well / and be taught by him himself / in their <term>inner being; the "Inwendigen," referring to the internal spiritual faculty or soul</term>. As is also witnessed in Isaiah 52:6, chapter 54:13, Jeremiah 31:31-34, John 6:45, Hebrews 8:10, 11.

To be sure, this is characteristic of the children of God at all times / and especially in the <term>New Covenant; the theological era beginning with Christ</term> / proper...

### The Fifth Little Law.

## Explanation and Proof.

I certainly believe that this speech of mine—wherein I say that God the Lord would only at such a time establish His Kingdom in the world or on the earth—might seem somewhat strange to some who are of the opinion that God already now has His Kingdom everywhere on earth, and especially in so-called <term>Christenheit: Christendom; the collective body of professing Christians</term>. It might make them eager to ask me: Whether I do not think that God already has His Kingdom in the world now? And whether the so-called Christian people are not then God’s people?

But to this I answer according to my knowledge, which the Lord has graciously bestowed upon me: that God the Lord does not yet have His Kingdom in the world now, but rather that the Devil, as a god and powerful prince of this world, has his wicked <term>Teufels-Reich: Devil’s Kingdom</term> everywhere in the whole world—and his work in the children of unbelief—and that he rules and governs among all the peoples of the earth, driving them into his service in all sorts of sins, shames, and vices. 

Likewise, the so-called Christian people are not God’s people, but rather a completely wicked race and an <term>Antichristisches Teufels-Volck: Antichristian devilish people</term>, being far worse than the <note>original: "Türcken oder Heyden"; "Turks or heathens." In the 17th century, "Turks" was often used as a general term for Muslims or the "other," while "heathens" referred to non-Abrahamic pagans.</note> themselves; for if they were God’s people, they would necessarily also have to serve Him as faithful subjects, and keep His commandments, and live and act accordingly, and proclaim the virtues of Him <note>This is a reference to 1 Peter 2:9: "that ye should shew forth the praises of him who hath called you out of darkness into his marvellous light."</note> who had called them out of darkness into His marvelous light. 

But because they do not do such things at all, but instead live in all sorts of quite obvious sins, shames, and <term>Laster: vices or depravities</term>, worse than the heathens themselves, they prove well enough by this that they are not God’s people, but an evil Antichristian devilish people, and that

### The Seventh Little Law.

Babel will fall deep into torment all at once with a great crash; then Zion will be built up on earth and prepared as a bride.

### Explanation and Proof.

When I wish to speak fundamentally of **Babel**, the great city and hideous <term>sorcerous harlot: Zauber-Huren</term> <note>A common Pietist and Radical Reformation term for the "false" institutional church, derived from the "Whore of Babylon" in the Book of Revelation.</note>, and of her fall and destruction—about which so much is written in **Holy Scripture**—according to its proper and broadest sense or understanding: then I say that by it must be understood the entire great **Kingdom of the Devil** on earth, and the **false church** among Christians, Jews, Turks, and Heathens.

Or, to say it more clearly: it refers to all fleshly, <term>unregenerate: unwiedergebohrne</term> <note>Those who have not experienced a spiritual "new birth" or internal transformation, a central concept in Pietist theology.</note> humans in the whole world, among Christians, Jews, <term>Turks: Türcken</term> <note>In the early modern period, "Turks" was frequently used as a general term for Muslims.</note>, and <term>Heathens: Heyden</term> <note>Non-Christian and non-Abrahamic peoples; often used to mean "Gentiles" or "pagans."</note>, with their formalized religions, superstitious ceremonies, and church-pomp or "divine services" (as they call it). For just as, in a certain measure, all spiritual, reborn humans are members of the **Body of Christ** and living stones of **Zion**, or the dwelling-place of **God** in the Spirit; so too are all fleshly, unregenerate humans in the whole world members of the impure body of the great Babylonian harlot, and stones—or even posts, beams, bolts, clay, and sand, etc.—of the great city of **Babel**, the dwelling or residence-city of the **Devil** in the flesh.

And when the fall and destruction of **Babel** is taught in general or overall, one must understand by it the fall of the entire false church and the destruction of the **Kingdom of the Devil** upon the whole earth, among all peoples of the world.

But because in such a golden time through Christ, sin shall be sealed up and an everlasting righteousness shall be brought into men (Daniel 9:24), the Dragon <note>A reference to Satan, as described in Revelation 20.</note> himself will lie sealed in the abyss, and the LORD God will be King over all lands, and will have His tent in Zion and His dwelling in Jerusalem among the children of men; then one will no longer know or hear of any devastation or any harmful weather <note>original: "schädlicher Witterung"; this refers to storms, frost, or droughts that ruin harvests, which were seen as part of the "curse" on the earth.</note> in the land, but all shall be well in the land, and the nations shall be blessed and favored by the Lord in every way, of which the scriptural passages cited above give a clear testimony.

### The Nineteenth Little Law.

O well for those who reach this time, when sorrow must give way to joy; when Jehovah <note>original: "Jehova"; the personal name of God in the Hebrew Bible, often used in this period to emphasize a direct, personal relationship with the Divine.</note> Himself appears without clouds to all people who will serve Him.

### Explanation and Proof.

In this little law, it is testified that those people who shall live to see such a time on earth shall be most highly blessed; which the angel also testified to Daniel in chapter 12:12, where he says: *Blessed is he who waits and reaches 1335 days* (namely the twelfth hour on the great world-workday <note>A theological concept where human history is divided into six "days" of a thousand years each; the "twelfth hour" refers to the final moments before the great Sabbath or Millennial reign.</note>, when the Lord will give the reward to His faithful servants and workers, in the time of refreshment before the face of the Lord) when all curses, sorrow, and grief are banished from the world, and the Lord will bless all nations and lands and do good to them, after He has become truly known and revealed to them, and they altogether serve Him in holiness and righteousness that is pleasing to Him. O yes, well to those, I say once more, who reach such a golden time! But yet those are far more blessed who have a part in the first resurrection, or also the transformation at the coming of Christ; for these enter with Him into the inner wedding-hall <note>A mystical term for the intimate union between Christ and the Church or the individual soul.</note> and enjoy, as it were, the kernel and the marrow of Divine goodness and the sweetness of love, and rule with Christ as Royal Priests over the outer church for such a thousand years (Revelation 20:4, 5, 6).

### The Twentieth Little Law.

Come then, you golden Rose! Your adornment pleases me better than
(Autumn-crocuses,
(Narcissi,
let me also truly enjoy you, and in you kiss my Beloved <note>A reference to the "Beloved" in the Song of Solomon, representing Christ.</note> forever and ever!

### Explanation and Proof.

In this last little law, I bring to light my longing, which I have for such a golden rose- or lovely-green-

--- Page 78 ---
<meta>This page concludes the author's explanation of a hymn regarding the "good time" (the Millennium). He transitions from the symbolic imagery of seasons to a list of deeper theological mysteries he has chosen not to reveal fully in this specific pamphlet.</meta>

...and blossoming spring and summer time; I testify that it pleases me far better than this present cold and harsh autumn-time (which I intended to signify with the autumn crocus or autumn flowers) or even all the beauty, pleasure, and magnificence of this world, which I intended to signify with the beautiful narcissi and lilies. I wish to enjoy that coming time so that I may kiss my most beloved Soul-Treasure <note>A Pietist term for Jesus Christ, emphasizing a personal, mystical union between the soul and the Savior.</note> in his inner bridal chamber in heartfelt love, and also enjoy his kiss, and hold the joyful wedding feast with him. Of which, however, nothing more can or may be reported here, because of the wicked mockers; and I only wish in closing that all those who read this may have their portion of such great joy and blessedness with me. Yes, Amen, so be it! Amen!

And this, beloved reader, is a very brief explanation and confirmation of what I have sung and said in this little song concerning the good, good time and the preceding judgments upon the godless world; and I hope that you will be well able to perceive and understand from this that I have brought forth nothing therein but what has its deep foundation in Holy Scripture. Yet no one should imagine that this is a complete report of such matters; no, it is not, for there are still many mysteries which belong as petals to this golden rose <note>The "Golden Rose" is the author’s metaphor for the unfolding of divine revelation or the perfected state of the Church in the end times.</note>, of which nothing or very little has been mentioned in this little work. These include: the mystery of the first resurrection; the awakening of the rams of Nebaioth <note>original: "Böcke Nebajoth"; a reference to Isaiah 60:7, here interpreted as a judgment or transformation of worldly tyrants and false clergy.</note> or the godless tyrants and the idol-priests; the reign of Christ and His saints on earth over these thousand years; likewise concerning Christ and His bride themselves, and the wedding guests; concerning the final deception of the heathens by Satan, as well as concerning Satan himself, and his binding and being loosed again; concerning the final resurrection of the dead and the general judgment; as well as the passing away of these old heavens and earth and the new creation of heaven and earth, and other deep mysteries besides. All of these must yet be incorporated as petals of this golden rose if it is to appear in its perfect form and beautiful ornament. But because I did not wish to bring forth anything new in this little work, but only to explain and strengthen that which I have already said before, all such mysteries have been passed over herein. However, if God gives grace, I will deal with them in another place and in a separate treatise, and provide a thorough and orderly report according to the measure of grace and knowledge that may be granted and communicated to me by God for that purpose.

# New Preface /
### concerning the second edition of this hymn / and the discourse set above it.

<margin>A 2</margin>

->Beloved Friends!<-

We read in the books of Moses <note>The first five books of the Old Testament, also known as the Pentateuch.</note> / that God the Lord gave his people, among others, these commandments: that / when they encountered their brother’s / or even their enemy’s oxen / or donkey or sheep straying / they should lead them back onto the right path / and bring them to their owner; and when they see their brother’s / or their hater’s ox / or donkey fall / and lie under its burden / they should be careful and not let it stay stuck under its burden and perish / but rather gladly set aside their own business / and help such poor animals up again / as can be read in Exodus 23:4–5 and Deuteronomy 22:1–4.

But regarding these words I might well ask / just as the holy Apostle Paul once asked / regarding the threshing ox in 1 Corinthians 9 / and say: Is God so concerned for the oxen and donkeys? And is his only concern / that no human should suffer loss through the loss of a piece of livestock? Or does he not rather say it for our sake / for it is indeed written for our sake.

For although it is certainly the case that the

<summary>This preface introduces the second edition of a hymn and sermon, using biblical laws about helping stray or burdened animals as a metaphor for spiritual care among neighbors.</summary>

--- Page 82 ---
<meta>This page continues the sentence from the previous page, completing the thought that while God cares for animals, His primary concern is the human soul.</meta>

gracious God and Creator of all things / still cares for the poor livestock / and gives them their fodder; and he also does not look kindly upon men / when they treat the poor animals with tyrannical blows / or overload them with heavy burdens and labor and do too much; and he also requires / that we should keep our neighbor free from harm in all things / as much as is within our power / and return all his lost property to him / whenever we find it: So I certainly believe / that God intended to teach and command us with these words / that we / should bring back his lost creatures to him / as the rightful Owner and Lord of all creatures; to call back the straying souls from their erring path / and point them to the right way of life / and faithfully stand by our poor, oppressed neighbor in every need and circumstance / according to our full ability: Just as the Holy Spirit indeed exhorts and drives us toward this on nearly every page of Holy Scripture: for truly, more depends upon a single soul / if it is lost / or if it is rescued and preserved from eternal destruction / than on a thousand oxen and donkeys. As Christ the Lord himself says / that the angels in heaven rejoice to the highest degree / over one sinner who performs penance <note>The German *Busse* carries the dual meaning of internal repentance and the outward act of penance.</note> / and whose soul is thus found and preserved / by which he gives us clearly enough to understand / how very much God and his holy angels and children care about the preservation and conversion of straying souls.

And these things are then all, in and of themselves, so holy and innocent / so just and also so highly necessary / that they have no equal / and they so far surpass all other **works of love** / which might yet be directed toward or spent upon the body / as the sun with its light, power, and warmth / surpasses the other planets and stars; for all other good works only concern the body / ensuring it is provided with all necessities / and preserved in this temporal life; but these concern the soul / that it may be rescued from eternal death and destruction / and be preserved with the body for eternal life; and just as much as the soul is nobler than the poor body / which belongs to decay / so much also are these **works of love** / which are applied to the conversion and preservation of souls / nobler than those / which are spent purely on the poor body alone.

one could also hardly believe / that a rational person would be found in the world / who would call such a good and truly Christian cause or deed evil / and seek to thwart it: if one did not perhaps already know / that the **Devil** has his kingdom in the world / and his work in the children of **unbelief** / and thus seeks / in and through them / to suppress all good and Divine work / and, as it were, to stifle it in its birth: but whoever knows this / and has perhaps also read the biblical and other good histories / will not be greatly surprised along with me / when he sees or hears / that such a good and Christian deed is not suffered or tolerated / but is opposed with all might / and denounced as evil / as I have personally experienced in fact / and wish to report here in only a few words.

For it has now been many / yes, more than twenty years / since the kind and merciful God in His singular **grace** thought of me, a poor little worm <note>A common expression of humility in 17th-century religious writing, emphasizing the insignificance of the individual compared to the greatness of God.</note> / and began to pull me out of my great misery and natural corruption of the soul / or **mire of sin** / and to set my feet upon the **way of peace**; and since then He has led me in secret along such a path / that is not walked by many these days / and has allowed me to taste and see many things / which remain hidden and unknown to many.

and that he who does evil cannot stand before Him; thus, He granted me a deep insight into the great **mystery of wickedness** <note>original: "Geheimnüs der Boßheit"; a reference to the "mystery of iniquity" in 2 Thessalonians 2:7, referring to the hidden power of sin at work in the world.</note> and the massive falling away of **nominal Christians** <term>nominal Christians: individuals who claim the name of Christ and belong to a church but do not live according to the spirit of the Gospel.</term> from God and Christ their Head. Because of this, I soon became a mockery to the people and an object of contempt; indeed, I became perhaps the most despised and worthless person in our entire village and region.

But when the Lord had so opened my eyes and shown me clearly how almost all flesh had corrupted its way, and how the shepherds and their flocks were walking on the path of destruction, I felt a deep pity for the great misery and ruin of these poor souls. And because the Lord had already given me grace for about fifteen years to express my earnest **penitential struggle** <term>penitential struggle: (German: *Buß-Kampf*) the intense internal spiritual conflict a believer undergoes while turning away from sin toward God.</term> and inward exercises of faith and love through various songs, rhymes, and heartfelt prayers, I also had them printed under the title of a *Spiritual Harp-Play of the Children of Zion*. I wished to show them that they must necessarily see, hear, taste, feel, and perceive the **Word of Life** <note>original: "Wort des Lebens"; a reference to Christ Himself as the living Word (John 1:1), emphasizing a personal, sensory experience of the divine.</note> or Christ within themselves. In doing this, I certainly acted with no improper intent; I did nothing more than what I was duty-bound to do, as my own heart and conscience still bear witness to me.

not only did the poor, blind people fall into a complete commotion / and for the benefit / which I intended to show them / they mocked, hated, and blasphemed me to the highest degree / and regarded me as if I were the very worst person in the world; but also the **Lords of the Superior Consistory** <term>Superior Consistory: (German: *Ober-Kirchen-Räthe*) the high-ranking administrative council of the state church responsible for doctrine and discipline.</term> / (who indeed consider themselves to sit in **Moses’ seat** <note>original: "Mosis Stul"; a reference to Matthew 23:2, implying those who hold positions of religious authority and law-giving.</note> / and thus should rightly have rejoiced / when they heard / that perhaps an **Eldad or Medad** <note>Eldad and Medad were two elders of Israel who prophesied in the camp rather than at the Tabernacle; Moses refused to silence them, saying "I wish that all the Lord's people were prophets" (Numbers 11:26-29).</note> was still prophesying in their entirely spiritless and godless military camp / if they had otherwise possessed the Spirit of Moses; ) immediately imposed upon me a total and, as it were, eternal silence / without telling me why?

<meta>This page concludes the author’s preface. The previous page ended with the author describing how his warnings of divine judgment were met with hostility, comparing his critics to those who refused to respond to the "trumpet of repentance."</meta>

...begin and hate me even more, blaspheme and persecute me, than has happened before. But regardless of this, I cannot refrain from warning them once more of their great harm and destruction.

For because the great error <note>German: *Irrsaal*. This term implies a state of being spiritually lost or wandering in confusion.</note> and danger in which they find themselves is known to me, and I know for certain that they are all together on the path they walk, falling into the jaws of eternal death if they do not turn back and do penance <term>penance: (German: *Busse*) In this context, it refers to a deep spiritual turning away from sin and toward God; a total change of heart.</term>, and since I have also personally tasted a little of the great anguish and distress that awaits unrepentant sinners <term>unrepentant sinners: (German: *unbußfertige Sünder*) individuals who refuse to acknowledge their failings or seek divine forgiveness.</term> together in hell, and thus know how very bitter it is, I have great compassion for them. I consider myself most deeply bound to reveal to them the great danger in which they find themselves, to warn them of harm, to exhort them toward the good, and also to teach or instruct them with a few words on which path they might yet escape their destruction.

But if they will not be warned, nor take counsel, but rather remain in their evil mind and stray path, then I am innocent of their blood or destruction, because I have done for them what I could, and what I was obligated to do for them. And this is what I have deemed advisable and necessary to mention here as a preface <note>German: *Vorrede*.</note>; for the rest, I refer the kind reader to the following little work <note>German: *Wercklein*. A humble diminutive often used by authors of this period to describe their books, suggesting the work is modest in size or ambition.</note> itself, and wish from my heart that reading through it might bring some benefit to his soul <note>German: *Seelen*.</note>.

U. the 14th of Nov. 1727.

->**E. S.**<-

<summary>The author concludes his preface by asserting his duty to warn others of spiritual danger, despite personal persecution, and offers his book as a guide for the reader's soul.</summary>
<keywords>repentance, persecution, spiritual danger, preface, compassion, salvation</keywords>

<meta>This page begins the "First Preface." The previous page concluded the author's general introduction where he expressed compassion for those in spiritual danger and justified his role as a messenger. This page transitions into the specific content of his message, which he frames metaphorically as a "New Song" of divine grace intended for all people regardless of their faith or status.</meta>

# First Preface.

Grace and peace / love and mercy / from God and from His Son Jesus Christ / be with all / who hear or read this! Yes, Amen!

->Beloved children of men!<-

You may be whoever you wish / Christians / Jews / Turks <note>In the early modern period, "Turks" was the standard term used to refer to Muslims.</note> or Heathens / young or old / great or small / rich or poor / and you may also dwell in all places and ends of the entire wide world wherever you wish.

I sing here with a joyful sound before your ears a **NEW SONG** / of the eternal love and grace of our so friendly and philanthropic <term>philanthropic: (German: *leutseeligen*) In this 17th-century context, this describes God’s kind, approachable, and gracious disposition toward humanity.</term> God and Savior Jesus Christ / which I have received from Him Himself / to the end / that I shall lay it before your eyes / and sing it before all your ears.

Now it is indeed certain / that this my song is very lovely and full of joy / and my voice is quite sweet / also the words / which I bring forth / are quite gracious and worthy of all acceptance / as well as entirely truthful and certain.

It is also certain / that this my song proclaims good to every creature / and nothing evil to anyone. Meanwhile, I can quite easily reckon / that it will not please everyone. And I do not doubt / there will still be those at this time / who will likely be more numerous / seeking to disturb me in my song / than there will be those / who [stand] by— <meta>The text cuts off here. The catchword "stim-" indicates the next page begins with a word like "stimmen" (to agree or harmonize).</meta>

<summary>The author opens his first preface by addressing all of humanity, regardless of religion or status, with a "New Song" of Christ's love. He claims this message was received directly from God and, while joyful and universal, he anticipates facing opposition from critics.</summary>
<keywords>Universalism, Grace of Christ, New Song, Religious Tolerance, Divine Revelation, Preface</keywords>

--- Page 92 ---
<meta>This page continues the "First Preface" from page 13. The previous page ended mid-sentence with "who [stand] by—", which this page completes with "accord." The author continues his musical metaphor of a "New Song" to describe his message of universal salvation.</meta>

accord / and with me to the praise of our **GOD** / sing this **New Song**. Because it is now such a time / when almost everyone prefers to hear the sound and blast of the trumpets and cannons <note>original: "Carthaunen" (Kartaune) — a type of heavy cannon used in the 16th and 17th centuries.</note> from the high walls of the city of Babylon <note>In Radical Pietist literature, "Babylon" often symbolized the established, corrupt state churches, contrasted with "Zion," the true spiritual community.</note> / rather than the sweet and lovely song and harp-sound in the stillness of Zion.

Yes! I also expect / that some / as soon as they even hear me / will dare / to attack me with blasphemous and abusive words / and to cry me out as a heretic / a straying spirit / and a visionary <term>visionary: (German: *Phantast*) In this period, the term was used pejoratively to describe someone whose religious ideas were considered wild, delusional, or disconnected from orthodox scripture.</term> / or perhaps even undertake something else against me.

But despite all that / I cannot keep silent about / what I have seen and heard. And therefore I sing my **New Song** of the great Trumpet- and Jubilee- or Year of Release <term>Year of Release: (German: *Erlaß-Jahr*) A reference to the biblical "Year of Jubilee" (Leviticus 25) where debts were forgiven and slaves freed; here used as a metaphor for the ultimate restoration of all souls to God.</term> of the **LORD** before the ears of every creature / with a confident heart and a joyful voice / and I allow everyone their freedom to judge it / as they please. And if it should please some to rise up against me with blasphemy and persecution / I grant them that freedom as well / and tell them beforehand / that against them / insofar as they only attack me and my person / I will defend myself with no other weapons / than loving / suffering / and enduring.

But insofar as they touch this truth / which I hereby testify / I will certainly not be found soft-hearted or despondent before them / but shall seek to represent it with all my ability / which **GOD** will grant me / and it might then well happen / that one or the other might be felled by his own sword and struck to the ground.

<summary>The author expresses his determination to proclaim a "New Song" of divine grace (the Jubilee Year) despite inevitable accusations of heresy or visionary delusions. He pledges to meet personal attacks with patience but to defend his theological truth with spiritual strength.</summary>
<keywords>New Song, Babylon vs. Zion, heresy, Year of Jubilee, spiritual warfare, patience, universal restoration</keywords>

--- Page 93 ---
<meta>This page concludes the "First Preface" dated June 1, 1725. It follows the author's declaration that he will speak his truth regarding universal salvation regardless of the "blasphemy and persecution" mentioned at the end of the previous page.</meta>

It would indeed have been an easy matter for me to have immediately fortified all the lines that I bring forth here, front and back, with scripture verses <note>original: "Schrifft-Sprüche" — citations from the Bible used to prove a theological point.</note>. However, I have considered it good to lay it quite bare before everyone's eyes for this time, just as I received it from **GOD**, like a little field flower whose color and scent everyone can enjoy according to their own pleasure. 

But if I should see that perhaps some quarrelsome Stoic philosophers <note>original: "Stoicker-Philosophi" — a term Schütz uses here to describe rigid, overly intellectual, or unemotional orthodox theologians who lack spiritual "warmth."</note> should set about it with disputing and seek to spoil it or make it loathsome to others; then I will not let the effort trouble me, and I will build such a wall around it that no little fox <note>A reference to Song of Solomon 2:15: "Take us the foxes, the little foxes, that spoil the vines."</note> nor wild boar <note>A reference to Psalm 80:13: "The boar out of the wood doth waste it," often interpreted as enemies of the true faith.</note> will be able to dig through it, but instead it will remain standing quite firm and immovable against all storms and attacks. This has now happened, to some extent, in the following explanation <term>explanation: (German: *Erläuterung*) referring to the main body of the text that follows this preface.</term>. With which I commend the reader to the healing grace of **GOD** and remain

### The one who loves his soul

Umstadt, June 1st, 1725.

### Christoph Schütz.

<summary>Schütz concludes his preface by explaining his choice to present his ideas simply, like a "field flower," while warning that he is prepared to defend his views with scripture if orthodox critics ("Stoic philosophers") attack him.</summary>
<keywords>scripture, simplicity, polemics, Christoph Schütz, Umstadt, 1725, defense of faith</keywords>

# A Song of the Eternal Grace, Love, and Mercy of GOD over All Creatures.

->To the melody: Wake up, the voice calls to us.<-
<note>original: "Wacht auf / rufft uns die Stimme" — a famous Lutheran hymn by Philipp Nicolai (1599). Using this melody suggests a call to spiritual awakening and the approaching of a new era of divine revelation.</note>

1. Behold, you peoples of all places! The Most High calls to you most faithfully with sweet words. Hear what He tells you, and what in these last days is still being made known to you by Him. It is such a word that, now and forevermore, gives something of great significance to Christians, Jews, Turks <note>In the 18th century, "Turks" was the standard term for Muslims.</note>, Heathens—to all people.

2. Yes! At this hour, a most righteous and true word proceeds from His precious mouth of truth, which shall remain eternally firm, even if many currently strive against it in every place. Yet it remains so: that He is Love, and makes all creatures <term>creatures: (German: *Creatur*) in this theological context, refers to all created beings, often including humans, animals, and even fallen spirits.</term> new; oh, only believe it! For this is His high oath.

3. In the beginning, He created all things—the great as well as the small—heartily, good, and beautiful; and it was His earnest will that His rich fullness of love should stand open to all things, and that everything, at all times, should love Him for His goodness and be thankful to Him, and together join in His praise.

4. This divine purpose <term>purpose: (German: *Fürsatz*) refers to God's original plan or decree for creation.</term> must stand! Even if—alas!—it has already happened, as is evident to everyone, that very many creatures have lost themselves through sin; and a great separation occurred, after sin [itself]...

<meta>catchword: itself (German: *sich*)</meta>

<summary>This page introduces a hymn celebrating the "Restoration of All Things," a belief that God’s love will eventually reclaim all of creation, regardless of religious background. The verses emphasize God's original intent for a perfect, harmonious creation as a promise that remains valid despite the reality of sin.</summary>

<keywords>universal salvation, divine love, creation, mercy, hymn, restoration, spiritual awakening, all nations</keywords>

...[sin] crept in like a thief, and separates God from His creatures, so that quickly the wrath is kindled within them.

5. Then they had no peace; yes, many raged as if mad, even standing against God and His Son—who exist in the greatest friendship <note>original: "in der grösten Freundschafft stehen"; this likely refers to the perfect, loving union between God the Father and the Son, which the rebellious "creatures" now oppose.</note>; instead, they inflict the greatest woes and unheard-of suffering upon themselves, because they used sin as their servant in all their slavery, and it plagued them with unheard-of tyranny.

6. Yes! In such great distress, in such agony and many deaths into which sin has brought them, they would have had to remain forever, and constantly perform the works of sin, because no one can redeem themselves by their own power. Oh! But listen: how God the Lord is so friendly at all times in His Son Jesus Christ.

7. For when His creature was corrupted and many had died to the life that came from Him, this brought many pains to His faithful heart, so that He was deeply troubled, and took counsel with Himself as to what deed and what kind of <term>wonder-cure: (German: *Wunder-Cur*) a miraculous medical treatment, here used metaphorically for the divine plan of salvation.</term> He might use to heal His <term>creature: (German: *Creatur*) refers to all created beings, including humans and the natural world.</term>

8. Then it was decided by Him that, through His favored companion and dearest Son Jesus Christ, He would renew all things and free everything that had been separated from Him from the curse and death. It was not too much for Him, and it was His very earnest will to give His heart’s-child <note>original: "Herzens-Kind"; a tender, Pietistic term for Jesus, emphasizing the emotional bond between the Father and the Son.</note> and dearest part for the world’s salvation, in great haste.

9. This pure will of love, His heart’s-child was ready and willing to fulfill at once, and he took this great work upon himself, and—

<meta>This page continues the hymn describing the divine plan for the restoration of all things. The opening fragment completes the sentence from the previous page where God gives His "heart’s-child" (Jesus) the authority to act.</meta>

...received power from the Father to make everything completely beautiful and new, and free from corruption, so that nothing more of the curse is recognized. And His hand brings everything back to its original state.

10. This one will now also reign and exercise lordship until all things are brought back that were ever lost, because He is chosen for this purpose: that through His power of love He shall complete this work. And when He has made everything good and new in every place, He quickly gives the rule back to His Father.

11. Therefore God now offers peace to every creature in Jesus Christ His Son; everything that was separated from Him shall be reconciled through Him and rest gently in His love. The sin that caused the separation shall be completely burned away, along with all its evil fruit, which God has cursed and seeks to utterly eradicate.

12. He wishes to harbor no enmity against His own work, but only against sin and the work of the Devil; He will engage in war until He has completely worn them down through the power and strength of His love. But His own work He will, in power and strength, make good and new once more, and entirely free from death and the Devil’s tyranny.

13. Everything that He did not create He will eradicate and snatch away, no matter what it might be; but His "very good" <note>original: "sehr gutes Wesen"; this likely refers to Genesis 1:31, where God surveys His creation and finds it "very good."</note> essence, which flowed out from Him, He will make free from evil. He will not allow even a tiny speck of dust to be lost, for because it was imperishable, it will become clear; yet the rust shall be completely burned away.

14. In the fallen angels and in humans full of defects, all of the Devil’s essence will return to its state of nothingness; but the good shall endure and become pure and clear once more, and

18. 19. When they then bow down thus, most humbly, and cry to the Lord for rescue from the torment and distress; when they convert to Him, then He will hear their cry and save them from the death that has gnawed on them for so long, and

26. O you peoples of all places, what are you doing that you so wickedly oppose your Creator with words and deeds, and injure your own heads like madmen who dash themselves against stones, causing themselves great pain and suffering in every moment <note>original: "nu"; an archaic term for a fleeting instant or the blink of an eye.</note>, and never having peace and rest.

27. Let yourselves be given the best advice! And leave your evil deeds, for you will truly not succeed! 28. 29. Love Him who loves you, and though you have greatly grieved Him, He still remains faithful and gracious to you, and gladly wants [to give] you life, yes also...

Rath" - literally "knows counsel," meaning He has a plan or solution for every situation.</note> in all things.

42. He is quite a wise master; / He knows and tests all spirits / as they are in their inner core; / He has water and also fire; / He can sweep the wheat clean from the chaff / even by His wind; / what cannot be washed / that He places on the test <term>Test|A cupel or refining vessel used by early modern chemists and goldsmiths to separate precious metals from impurities</term> / and heats it most faithfully / until it is pure and the dross <term>Schlacken|The waste material or scum left over after metal has been smelted</term> is burned away.

43. 44. If you then now want to persist / in your stubborn mind / like fools / then be assured and certain / that He will yet with many blows <note>original: "Schlägen" - implies divine discipline or the "stripes" of correction.</note>

§. 3. For he—the great **God of Love** and **Creator**—created all things in the beginning through **Christ** <note>original Latin: "Christum"</note>, his living **Word** (John 1:3; Colossians 1:16), and they were indeed "very good" (Genesis 1:31). And it was his earnest will that all his works and <term>creatures (Geschöpfe): all living beings brought into existence by God</term> should be and remain with him in a good and blessed <term>well-being (Wohlstand): a state of spiritual and physical prosperity</term>, enjoying his great goodness and the sweetness of his love; they were meant to recognize, love, praise, honor, and glorify him as their **God**, **Creator**, and kind benefactor without end.

§. 4. And this plan and holy purpose of the **Lord** must now also stand (Isaiah 46:10), and this holy will of his must certainly happen and be fulfilled once more. Because his counsel remains eternally, and the thoughts of his heart endure from generation to generation (Psalm 33:11). This remains true even though it has—alas!—already happened, to **God’s** greatest displeasure, that very many of his noble works and creatures have been separated from him and lost through sin, falling prey to eternal death, curse, and destruction.

§. 5. For as soon as sin, like a wicked thief, had crept in and acted as an evil <note>original: "Mittelwand"; a "middle wall" or dividing barrier, referencing the separation between the sacred and the profane or between God and humanity</note> separating **God** and his creatures, those same creatures—torn away or separated from **God** by sin—found themselves in the greatest unrest, distress, misery, and destruction, under **God’s** curse and condemnation. They had no peace at all, neither with **God** nor within themselves. Just as even at this hour all the ungodly have no peace, but are like a raging sea that cannot be still, casting up its waves, mire, and filth; yes, they allow themselves to be led by sin—which holds them captive in their own defiance—to all its evil service and work...

<summary>The author argues that God's original "very good" creation and his eternal purpose for the well-being of all creatures must ultimately prevail over the temporary separation caused by sin. He describes sin as a thief and a dividing wall that brings misery and unrest to the ungodly, comparing their state to a turbulent sea.</summary>

And because **God** is **Love**, and loves all his works; is kind to all; and has mercy on all his works (Psalm 145:9); so then he will most certainly seek out and make **blessed** <term>blessed (seelig/selig): in this context, it refers to being "saved" or restored to a state of eternal happiness and divine favor</term> through **Christ** <note>original: "Christum"</note> all those who were lost, corrupted, and damned through sin. --- Page 113 ---
<meta>This page follows the discussion of God’s nature as Love and His intent to restore all creatures. The catchword "als" (as) at the bottom of the previous page suggests the transition "as he is their beginning [and end]."</meta>

### §. 3.
For He, the great **God of Love** and **Creator**, created all things in the beginning through **Christ** <note>original: "Christum"</note>, His living Word (John 1:3; Colossians 1:16), and indeed created them very good (Genesis 1:31). And it was His earnest will that all His works and creatures should be and remain with Him in a good and **blessed well-being** <term>well-being (Wohlstand): in this historical religious context, it refers not to material wealth, but to a state of spiritual health, peace, and flourishing in God’s presence</term>, enjoying His great goodness and the sweetness of His love, and should recognize, love, praise, honor, and glorify Him as their **God** and **Creator**, and as their kind Benefactor without end.

### §. 4.
And this plan and holy purpose of the **Lord** must then also stand firm (Isaiah 46:10), and this holy will of His must certainly come to pass and be fulfilled once more. For His counsel remains eternally, and the thoughts of His heart from generation to generation (Psalm 33:11). This is true even though, unfortunately, it has already happened—to God’s greatest sorrow—that very many of His noble works and creatures have been separated and lost from Him through sin, and have fallen prey to eternal death, curse, and ruin.

### §. 5.
For as soon as sin, like a wicked thief, had crept in alongside and, like an evil **wall of separation** <note>The "wall of separation" (Mittelwand) is a biblical allusion to Ephesians 2:14, representing the barrier of sin that divides humanity from the Divine.</note>, had divided **God** and His creatures, those same creatures—torn away or separated from God through sin—found themselves in the greatest unrest, distress, misery, and ruin, under God's curse and damnation; they had no peace at all, neither with **God** nor within themselves. Just as even at this hour all the ungodly have no peace, but are like a raging sea that cannot be still, casting up its waves, mire, and filth (Isaiah 57:20); indeed, they allow themselves to be led—by the sin that holds them captive in their own self-will—to all its evil service and work,

<summary>This page argues that because God created all things to be "very good," His original plan for the happiness and salvation of His creatures must eventually prevail over the temporary separation and misery caused by sin.</summary>

and are used as **slaves**, and they contend against the holy and righteous **God** constantly with their evil, sinful life and nature, like senseless beasts. In doing so, however, they gain nothing more than plunging themselves ever more and deeper into eternal death and damnation, and drawing upon themselves more and greater sufferings, agony, and pain in hell.

### §. 6.
And in such a most unhappy state and great need, misery, and ruin, they would then have had to remain forever, and serve sin as bondservants (Romans 6:16, 20). This is because no one can, by their own power, nor can one brother free another from the **bondage** of sin and the **Devil**, save them from death, or redeem them from hell; for it costs far too much, so that he must let it remain undone forever (Psalm 49:8–9 <note>In many English Bibles, this corresponds to Psalm 49:7–8.</note>). And **Christ** the **Lord** alone has the keys of hell and death (Revelation 1:18). He alone can reconcile **God** and—through His blood, as the blood of the covenant and the eternal testament—release His prisoners from the pit in which there is no water (Zechariah 9:11; 2 Corinthians 5:18–19; 1 John 2:2, chapter 4:10).

But oh, listen now, you dear people, how **God** the **Lord** is at all times so very kind, loving, and friendly in **Christ Jesus**, His dear Son.

### §. 7.
For when His noble creatures were thus corrupted through sin, and many angels—together with the entire human race in **Adam**—had died to the Divine and blessed life of joy, and with the whole outer world had fallen prey to the curse and ruin, then this grieved and pained Him very much in His heart full of love and mercy. ### §. 8.
Because, however, no creature—neither in heaven, nor on earth, nor under the earth—was found worthy or capable of healing such great damage and redeeming the poor fallen creature from their curse and ruin to make them blessed, except only the slain Lamb of God (Revelation 5 <note>original: *Apoc. 5*</note>). Therefore, it was determined by Him that He would begin, mediate, and complete such a high work Himself through His companion in grace and beloved only-begotten Son, **Jesus Christ**; and that He would seek out and save the entire fallen creation, or world, through Him. Indeed, it was not too much for Him; rather, it was His earnest will and gracious pleasure to give this most beloved Child of His heart <note>German: *Herzens-Kind*</note>—in whom He takes His entire delight—to the whole corrupted world and all fallen creatures as their salvation and Savior. Through Him, He intended to raise them all up together, or entirely, out of their fall and ruin, to free them from all distress and misery, to renew them, and to bring them back into their original good state.

### §. 9.
And then this holy Lamb and most beloved Child of God the Father’s heart was also—both out of love and obedience to **God** His Father, as well as out of love and mercy-filled compassion for the poor fallen creatures—immediately quite willing and ready to fulfill such a pure will of love from the Father toward His fallen works; and He took up this great work

<meta>This page continues the moral exhortations from page 51. The opening word "away" (ab) completes a command to turn away from sin. The text addresses common excuses for not living a holy life and emphasizes the necessity of spiritual rebirth.</meta>

...away and do good; die completely to sin and live for righteousness; strip off the old man <term>old man (alten Menschen): a biblical concept from the letters of Paul referring to the sinful, unregenerate nature of humanity</term> entirely and put on the new, who is created after God in true righteousness and holiness of truth; put your ungodly nature completely away from the eyes of God and let the Lord Jesus create an entirely new and upright nature in you according to his will, and conduct your whole life and actions as God the Lord demands of you in his word.

Yes, you say: "Who will then be able to live this way, as God requires in his word? For we are poor sinners and weak humans, and even when we want to do good, evil is right there with us," Romans 7. "And if we were supposed to keep the law ourselves, what would the merit of Christ <term>merit of Christ (Verdienst Christi): the theological idea that Christ's perfect life and sacrifice are credited to the believer, providing salvation that human effort cannot achieve</term> benefit us?" and so forth. But hear, you dear people! and know this: that all these excuses of yours are not worth even a penny <note>original: "Heller"; a small German coin of very little value, used here to illustrate that these excuses are worthless.</note> before God; and that such flimsy supports, with which you always prop up your old evil self, will one day all break and pierce your hands or hearts.

For if we are by nature children of wrath <term>children of wrath (Kinder des Zorns): a reference to Ephesians 2:3, describing the state of humanity as being subject to divine judgment due to innate sinfulness</term>, and born of flesh from flesh, then as such we cannot inherit the Kingdom of God, 1 Corinthians 6:9. Instead, we must be born anew from God as children of God, fashioned and shaped according to God, if we wish to enter the Kingdom of God, John 3:3, 5. If we are weak in ourselves and can do nothing good in our own strength, then through Christ we are capable of all things, and can do nothing evil nor sin, when we are born of God, 1 John 3.

§. 39. Oh, you wayward and utterly ill-natured children! You hypocrites and wicked sinners all together! §. 40. Yet you have your own will! You may follow the faithful counsel and heartfelt admonition of the **Lord** and come as soon as possible, and with your sincere return, fulfill the longing and the joy of **God** and His holy children; and thus seize your own happiness and eternal well-being from this moment onward. Or, you may remain outside even longer and persist in your sinful life and <term>nature</term> <note>original: "Wesen"; refers here to one's fundamental state of being or character.</note>, and thus spend, pass, or waste your noble life-time and strength as the evil spirit suggests—the one who works within you as children of unbelief, and whom you have served so faithfully until now. Everything is left free to you; you may do what you wish and choose for yourselves what seems best to you. For the dear **God** sets before you both His blessing and the curse—yes, heaven and hell, life and death—and truly desires with His whole heart that you should choose life and blessing. He counsels, admonishes, asks, and begs you even now, in this hour, through me—His poor child and utterly unworthy <term>little servant</term> <note>original: "Knechtlein"; the use of the diminutive "-lein" is characteristic of Pietist humility, emphasizing the speaker's smallness before God.</note>—that you should choose His blessing and life; yet He does not force you with great power, because He does not take back the noble gift of your <term>free will</term> <note>original: "freyen Willens"; a central theological concept here, arguing that while God desires salvation for all, He respects human agency.</note> with which He gifted and ennobled you at the beginning of <term>Creation</term> <note>original: "Schöpfung".</note>. And so, you may now choose according to that same will what seems good to you, and then eat the fruits of your choice and your works, whether they be good or evil.

<meta>This page continues the direct appeal to the reader's free will started on page 63. Having presented the choice between blessing and curse, the author now describes the divine reaction to each choice, emphasizing that while humans are free to choose destruction, God’s seeking love remains persistent. The section marker §. 4. likely serves as a sub-point within the broader exhortation.</meta>

§. 4.

If you now choose life and blessing, then know that the **Lord** our **God**, along with the entire heavenly host, rejoices greatly over you and your wise choice and great happiness; and come then with all haste and speed and seize such a thing (I mean the eternal life of joy and the blessing of **God**, together with your true salvation and great happiness) in **Christ Jesus**, as the beloved and only-begotten Son of the **Father**, in whom alone it is to be seized; and enjoy the good things in the land of the living.

But if you indeed wish to be so mad and foolish and choose the eternal curse and death instead of life and blessing, then do so at your own peril, and continue to run upon your perverse, evil way, even into the abyss of eternal destruction! Only know this: that such great folly will one day cause you much regret when you actually come to taste the fruits of your evil life and <term>nature</term> <note>original: "Wesen"; refers here to one's essential character and manner of living.</note> in their great bitterness; and that you have absolutely no excuse for such folly, because the good and faithful **God** warned you enough of your harm, but you did not wish to be warned or advised. Also, know this further: that the merciful **God** is grieved by your great folly and great misfortune, and yet, despite all this, He loves you constantly insofar as you are His creatures—though not in your devilish <term>wickedness</term> <note>original: "Unart"; implies a straying from one's original, intended nature.</note>—and He never gives you up as entirely lost. Instead, through His eternal Spirit and the great <term>Arch-Shepherd</term> <note>original: "Ertz-Hirten"; a reference to Christ as the primary caretaker of souls.</note> of the sheep, **Christ Jesus**, He will constantly and faithfully seek you until He has found you again and incorporated you into His holy flock, whether it happens sooner or later. <note>This conclusion suggests a "Universalist" or "Restorationist" theology, common among Radical Pietists, which posits that God's seeking love eventually reaches every soul, even beyond the grave.</note>

<summary>The author concludes the exhortation by contrasting the joy of heaven over a soul’s repentance with the bitter, but ultimately not final, consequences of choosing a sinful path. It emphasizes that Christ, as the Arch-Shepherd, continues to seek the lost until they are restored to the flock.</summary>

§. 41. For if He does not succeed with you in one way, and you do not wish to <term>turn back</term> <note>original: "bekehren"; often translated as repent or convert, implying a fundamental change of direction in one's life.</note> and draw near to Him when He so kindly calls and entices you, and invites you to the enjoyment of His love; but instead you despise His faithful counsel and heartfelt admonition, and cast them to the wind, and persist even more in your straying and sinning: then He already knows other <term>counsel and deeds</term> <note>original: "Rath und That"; a common German idiom suggesting both the plan and the power to execute it.</note>, and by which ways and means He can bring you to the point where you learn to seek Him and your own salvation and well-being, and to live by His grace. §. 42.
