He also has and uses fire, water, and wind for the purification of His corrupted works, and can thus, through His wind, very well separate and sweep away the chaff from His wheat; and through the water He washes away the impurity which would not allow itself to be swept away by His wind. But whatever also refuses to be washed and purified with His lye and <term>soap</term> <note>original: "Säuffe"; an archaic term for a washing solution or soap.</note>, He then places upon the <term>test</term> <note>original: "Test"; in metallurgy, this refers to a cupel or crucible used to refine precious metals by separating them from lead and other impurities.</note> or into His hot smelting-furnace, and then heats it up quite faithfully, and leads it through all seven trials by fire, and refines it so long until it is entirely pure, and all dross and impurities are completely burned away.

<summary>This is the title page for the third volume of Christoph Schütz’s "The Golden Rose," a treatise defending the doctrine of universal restoration (Apokatastasis). # Preface.

### Beloved Reader in God of eternal Love!

It would certainly be an absurd matter if I wished to attach a long preface to this subsequent little work, which is but a small epistle <note>The original term *Send-Brief* refers to a formal letter intended to be circulated or published like a pamphlet</note>; yet I consider it advisable and necessary to mention a few more things regarding it. And because at the time I composed this little work, I wrote a letter to a certain preacher—the very one who prompted me to write this work—in which almost everything I consider necessary to mention in a preface is contained, I will first place that letter here. Afterward, I will add only a few more things, as much as I judge necessary. That same letter reads as follows:

### Beloved Heart and Friend!

It is well known to you what a great clamor and uproar there has been for some time in our region among our countrymen concerning the doctrine of the <term>Restoration of All Things: Also known by the Greek term *apokatastasis*, this is the theological belief that all beings—including the damned—will eventually be reconciled with God and saved.</term> This began because Pastor Sch. <note>likely an abbreviation for a specific contemporary clergyman</note> gave a public testimony of it from the pulpit, and for that reason, by almost everyone

<summary>In this preface, the author addresses the controversy surrounding a local pastor's sermon on Universalism. He introduces the work by sharing a letter written to the man who encouraged him to write this defense of the "Restoration of All Things."</summary>

<keywords>preface, Restoration of All Things, universalism, Pastor Sch., pulpit, theological controversy, Pietism, circular letter</keywords>

--- Page 168 ---
<meta>This page continues the sentence from the previous page regarding the public reaction to Pastor Sch.’s sermon on the Restoration of All Things. The previous page ended with "everyone," which leads into the first word of this page, "one."</meta>

one was slandered and regarded and decried as a great evildoer.

Now, I did indeed have the hope that perhaps one of his colleagues, or brothers in office and good friends, would step to his side to rescue his innocence and seek to represent the truth that he had recognized and acknowledged; however, I now find myself disappointed in such hope. I hear that one of these friends says: the doctrine of the **Restoration of All Things** <note>original: *Wiederbringung aller Dinge*</note> is indeed a precious truth, but one must nevertheless remain silent (perhaps out of concern for losing one's honor and income if one testifies to it). Another says: one could not find any proper basis for it in Holy Scripture, and it is therefore very doubtful whether it is even a truth; the third calls it quite bluntly an erroneous and false doctrine and sets himself against it with both mouth and pen. And those who mean the best say: Pastor Sch. <note>likely Pastor Schütz or another contemporary proponent of Universalism</note> had nonetheless failed terribly by bringing such a doctrine to the pulpit and preaching it publicly to his congregation; for such food or doctrine was not fit for the common people and only made them more secure <note>In a theological context, "secure" (*sicherer*) meant "complacent" or "falsely confident in one's salvation," leading to a lack of moral effort</note> and more godless—along with other such judgments passed both upon this dear man and upon the aforementioned doctrine.

And because G. S. told me a few days ago that he had been with your Honor <note>The original *E. L.* stands for *Euer Liebden*, a formal and affectionate honorific common in 17th-century German correspondence, similar to "Your Kindness" or "Your Honor"</note> along with Pastor P.'s daughter, and that this matter was also discussed there, and your Honor had also said that Pastor Sch. had failed terribly by bringing such a doctrine to the pulpit, etc.; therefore, I immediately resolved to write a small letter regarding this matter

to write it. It is indeed a kind of food which in some places is quite sharply salted <note>The author uses "sharply salted" as a metaphor for harsh truths or confrontational language that might be difficult for critics to swallow</note>, and thus will likely not go down very smoothly for many, but rather will bite them on the tongue so that they might twist it like a snake, and might discharge the most poisonous arrows of slander from it and shoot them at me; but because the Lord Jehovah—whose truth I hereby witness and whose honor I seek to rescue through this—is himself with me, and his truth is my protection and shield, I fear no misfortune at all, but am quite joyful and of good cheer, and sing with the Psalmist: The right hand of the Lord gains the victory! Yes, the right hand of the Lord is exalted in Jesus! The right hand of the Lord nonetheless gains the victory over death and the devil, and his entire following.

Yes, see, my dear friend, not only do all these passages cited by me here, and many more like them, give a very clear and explicit testimony of such precious truth—the **Restoration of All Things** <note>original: *Wiederbringung aller Dinge*; a theological concept also known as *apocatastasis*, suggesting that all of creation will eventually return to a state of harmony with God</note>—but this testimony is even confirmed on every leaf of the entire Holy Scripture; as I have already said once, for as many words and testimonies as one finds therein which testify of the **Wisdom, Justice, and Omnipotence** <note>original: *Weißheit/ Gerechtigkeit und Allmacht*</note>, as well as of the **Grace, Love, and Mercy** <note>original: *Gnade/ Liebe und Barmherzigkeit*</note> of God, just as many words and testimonies one also finds which confirm this truth, because it is, as it were, the center or the focal point of all such divine attributes, and without which they could have no basis or place in God (as I have demonstrated more extensively in the following little work).

<meta>The catchword "keit" from the previous page completes the word "placidity" or more likely "righteousness" or "mercy" (*Barmherzigkeit*), following the list of divine attributes. ...to believe otherwise than that they have conducted their searching of the Scriptures regarding such matters in the way already mentioned, which method, however, is merely a threshing of empty straw and husks, by which one never finds a sweet kernel, nor arrives at the true foundation and the complete certainty and knowledge of a matter. For the **natural man** <term>natural man: original *natürliche Mensch*, a Pauline term (1 Cor 2:14) referring to a person relying on human reason alone, unassisted by the Holy Spirit</term> perceives nothing of that which is of the Spirit of God; indeed, it is even a foolishness to him, and he cannot know it, for it must be **spiritually discerned**. But the Spirit and the spiritual man search all things, even the depths of the Godhead, as St. Paul also testifies in 1 Corinthians 2.

And thus the true and thorough knowledge of this wholesome doctrine—as well as of all other divine truths and mysteries—can never be obtained through one’s own self-willed searching in **natural scholarship** <note>academic or secular learning practiced without spiritual devotion</note>, but rather through heartfelt prayer and total surrender to the will of God.

For our Savior says in one place (John 14:13–14): *Whatever you ask in my name, that I will do, so that the Father may be honored in the Son.* And since the highest honor of the Father is included in this doctrine, He will certainly not refuse its knowledge to anyone, nor let it remain hidden from those who ask Him for it in humility. But apart from this way, it is impossible to come to any thorough knowledge of the mysteries and wonders of God; for the **secret of the Lord** is only with those who fear Him, and the wisdom of this world is foolishness to God, as the Scripture says.

I do not exactly despise natural scholarship—

<summary>The author argues that the "Restoration of All Things" cannot be understood through dry academic study, but requires spiritual discernment and humble prayer. He cites Pauline and Johannine scripture to claim that God reveals His deepest mysteries only to those who surrender to His will rather than relying on human reason.</summary>
<keywords>Scriptural discernment, natural man vs spiritual man, 1 Corinthians 2, John 14, divine mysteries, humility in study, natural scholarship</keywords>

<meta>The catchword from the previous page, "keit," completes the word "righteousness" or "godliness." The author continues his defense of spiritual insight over mere intellectualism, distinguishing between truly pious scholars and those who use "reason" to attack divine truth.</meta>

...ness. No; for I hold in high regard truly learned people who, alongside their **natural scholarship** in the Scriptures and languages, are also true **divines** <term>divine: a scholar of God or a theologian (original: *Gottes-Gelehrte*)</term> and are inwardly taught by the **anointing** <note>original: *Salbung*; a reference to 1 John 2:27, implying the Holy Spirit’s direct guidance</note>. Rather, my speech is only against that puffed-up knowledge of **human reason** <term>reason: original *Vernunfft*, here referring to human intellect operating without spiritual illumination</term> and "serpent-wits" <note>original: *Schlangen-Witze*; a reference to the deceptive cunning of the biblical serpent</note>, which is the **wisdom of this world**. Such wisdom runs directly against Divine Wisdom and can never stand beside it, any more than light can stand beside darkness—a point to be observed in the following little work.

And if in several places I speak somewhat harshly against the learned, I do not wish to be understood as referring to all the learned without distinction. Instead, my speech is only against those over-clever gentlemen and **"ortho-toxic" quarrel-spirits** <note>original: *orthotoxische Zanck-Geister*; a pun mocking those who use "orthodoxy" as a weapon for brawling or disputes</note> who have sucked all their wisdom and scholarship from the breasts of the wild, chattering, yet ignorant **harlot** <note>likely an allusion to "Woman Folly" in Proverbs 9:13–18 or the "Whore of Babylon" in Revelation</note> who sits on her chair in the middle of the city. In such foolish worldly wisdom, they oppose the Wisdom of God with all their might and constantly seek to dispute and uproot it. Yes, my speech is against such as these; but it does not apply at all to truly pious men learned in the Scriptures and in God.

Furthermore, I hope that **Your Graces** <note>original abbreviation: *E. L.* (*Eure Liebden*), a respectful form of address to the reader or a patron</note> will not misinterpret my free and sincere style of writing; for "he who flatters his neighbor spreads a net for his footsteps," as that wise man says in **Proverbs 29:5**. However, if you or anyone else should find something in my accompanying little treatise that is offensive or not understandable enough, I ask that you take note of it

<summary>The author clarifies that his criticism is aimed at arrogant, quarrelsome theologians rather than truly pious scholars, citing spiritual anointing as the only true source of wisdom. He defends his blunt writing style as a form of biblical sincerity intended to avoid the traps of flattery.</summary>
<keywords>scholarship, divine wisdom, human reason, polemic, orthodoxy, piety, flattery, Proverbs 29:5</keywords>

**Umstadt, October 5th,**

### Christoph Schütz.

And this, beloved reader, is the very letter which I wrote three years ago to that preacher <term>preacher: a minister or member of the clergy (original: *Prediger*)</term> who—in the manner reported therein—prompted me to the composition of the following little work. And because that which I deemed necessary to mention in a preface <term>preface: an introductory explanation (original: *Vorrede*)</term> has already been sufficiently touched upon in that letter, I shall let the matter rest there. 

I will only mention further that shortly after I had sent this writing to that man, I learned that he had opposed my writing with another of about ten sheets <note>original: *10. Bogen*; in 18th-century printing, a "sheet" or "quire" was a large piece of paper folded into multiple pages, suggesting a rebuttal of roughly 80 to 160 pages</note> and had thus refuted it. Whereupon I, through opportunity and good acquaintances, several times requested that he should communicate his writing and refutation of mine to me, just as I had sent mine to him. However, I have not yet set eyes upon it to this hour, and I do not know whether he was perhaps ashamed that he, as a learned man and preacher, should engage in a written exchange <term>written exchange: a formal debate through letters or published pamphlets (original: *Schrifft-Wechsel*)</term> with me, a poor and despised layman <term>layman: a person without formal theological training or clerical office (original: *Leye*)</term>; or whether his own writing did not please him as well as it might have, and he the same...

<summary>Christoph Schütz concludes a 1721 letter to a preacher and explains that although the preacher wrote a lengthy rebuttal to his work, he has refused to share it, possibly due to the social stigma of debating a "simple layman."</summary>
<keywords>Christoph Schütz, Umstadt, 1724, preface, religious debate, layman vs. clergy, correspondence, pamphlet culture</keywords>

This is because it is not only a truth which has its deepest foundation in Holy Scripture and is therein very clearly and distinctly witnessed in many places, but it is even the singular crown and ornament of all other truths and Christian doctrines of faith in the whole Holy Scripture; without which the other primary truths and most precious doctrines of faith altogether—if one were to separate this from them—would be entirely misshapen.

For see, beloved friends! Firstly, Holy Scripture witnesses to us that GOD the Almighty Lord and Creator of all things had created all things through Christ, and indeed very well. And this is then also a precious truth; and if one now unites such a doctrine or truth of the creation of all things with the doctrine and truth of the Restoration of all things <note>original: *Wiederbringung aller Dinge*; the theological concept of universalism or apocatastasis, which posits that all fallen souls and creatures will eventually be returned to a state of perfection in God</note>, then the same appears in its perfect beauty and heartfelt adornment. And this latter is the former’s only crown and ornament; but if one wished to separate it from it, or deny it, then indeed the former—namely the doctrine or truth—

--- Page 178 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's argument that the doctrine of the "Restoration of All Things" is the essential framework that gives beauty and meaning to all other Christian dogmas. He argues that without this universalist perspective, the traditional teachings on creation and redemption appear broken or incomplete.</meta>

[the precious] truth of the creation of all things [would take on] a completely abominable form; because it would seem as though the entire world, with many millions of God's noblest creatures, were fundamentally corrupted, and to God's greatest vexation and disgust, lay under a curse and ruin. But when these same things are brought back through Christ and restored to their first good state, then God has pleasure in all His works, and all the works of the Lord will then praise Him and proclaim His glory forever and without end. Furthermore, Scripture teaches us concerning Jesus Christ that He is the Savior and Redeemer <note>original: *Heyland und Seeligmacher*; "Heyland" (Savior) implies one who heals or brings safety, while "Seeligmacher" (Redeemer/Bringer of Bliss) refers to one who grants eternal happiness.</note> not only of all humanity but also of the entire world; and this is then certainly a precious truth, and when one unites it with the doctrine of the Restoration of All Things <note>The "Restoration of All Things" (*Apocatastasis*) is the belief that all created beings, including the fallen, will ultimately be reconciled to God.</note>, it then appears in its perfect beauty and adornment. 

But if one wished to separate it from that, it would be found that Christ the Lord were not the Savior even of every thousandth person, let alone of the whole world. Thus, this doctrine of truth—which witnesses that Christ the Lord is the Savior of all people and also of the whole world—would certainly be so mutilated that not the slightest beauty, form, or shape would appear in it.

And just as it is with these precious primary doctrines of the Christian religion, which I have mentioned here—and how they would have not the slightest beauty, form, or shape, and would be very fragile if one were to rob them of the doctrine of the Restoration of All Things, or deny it and wish to separate it from them—exactly so it is with all others...

<meta>This page continues the author's argument that the doctrine of the "Restoration of All Things" is the essential framework for all Christian theology. [the] precious truths and doctrines of faith <term>original: *Glaubens-Lehren*; the formal teachings or dogmas of the church.</term> of the Christian religion; and they would certainly all be very fragile, and together have no form nor beauty, if one wanted to rob them of the doctrine of the Restoration of All Things <note>original: *Wiederbringung aller Dinge*; the belief that all created beings, including the fallen, will ultimately be reconciled to God.</note>. I would be willing to clearly prove and demonstrate all of this if I were to take up one doctrine of faith after another; but because I gladly strive for brevity, and also hope that for the sensible and truth-loving reader—through the two cited doctrines of God as the Creator of all things, and of Christ as the Savior <note>original: *Heyland*; a term emphasizing Christ as a healer and bringer of safety/salvation.</note> of the whole world—the door has already been opened and the path paved wide enough for further reflection and self-knowledge regarding such matters.

Thus, I will let it rest with this short instruction, and I hope the sensible reader will be able to perceive quite well from it what a highly precious truth and valid doctrine of faith this doctrine of the Restoration of All Things is, and how much depends on its right knowledge. Indeed, almost no one can thoroughly understand a single Christian doctrine of faith if he does not also thoroughly understand and perceive this doctrine and precious truth of the Restoration of All Things.

And because this doctrine is then not only a truth, but also (according to the reasons cited before) can even with the greatest justification be called a "truth of all truths"—yes, the highest crown and most beautiful ornament of all other truths and Christian doctrines of faith—I consider it well worth the effort, and permitted to me, that I [defend it] according to my full capacity, which the Lord grants me for it...

May you all be commended to God!

->C. S.<-

### Beloved Gentlemen and Friends!

Namely all of you, who do not yet know the great **God of Love**, in His infinite power and quality of love according to His holy and merciful decree <term>original: *Erbarmungs-vollen Rathschluß*; a theological term for God's pre-ordained plan of salvation.</term> and loving will toward all His creatures, but instead hold the conceit <note>original: *Einbildung*; here meaning a false mental image or a stubborn delusion.</note> of Him that He is such a hostile, angry, and irreconcilable **GOD**, who would never again allow Himself to be reconciled with those creatures whom He had once condemned to hell according to His punitive justice <term>original: *Straff-Gerechtigkeit*; the aspect of divine justice focused solely on retribution.</term>, nor grant them any grace, even if He had already avenged Himself upon them a thousand times over for their crimes; but would instead let them suffer in an entirely unspeakable agony and pain for as long as He is **GOD**—that is, without end. 

And because of this, you also—with mouth and pen, yes, even with sword, spear, and shield—so defiantly oppose the light and reflection of eternal truth that is newly rising in this dark evening-time <note>A common Pietist metaphor for the current age, suggesting a period of spiritual darkness just before a new divine revelation or the end of the world.</note>, wherein the infinite **God of Love** wishes to reveal Himself to the children of men and make Himself known as He is; and you absolutely will not believe or tolerate that such a kind **God of Love** and Father of Mercy <term>original: *Barmherzigkeit*.</term> should have mercy on all His works, and be kind to all, and—according to His great love and faithfulness—redeem them from their great misery and corruption and make them blessed <term>original: *seelig machen*; to bring to a state of eternal salvation and happiness.</term>: I wish you, from this great, gracious **God of Love** and Father of Mercy, much grace, love

->Third Part.<-

<page-num>22</page-num>

But because <term>Holy Scripture<note>original: *Heil. Schrifft*</note></term> is for the most part still a sealed book to them, and their eyes also blink (or are very weak and dim), they have therefore—like <term>Balaam<note>A biblical figure; the author refers to Numbers 24:6, which describes tents spread out like gardens by a river. Here, he uses it to suggest that theologians have settled by the "waters of reason" rather than the source of the Spirit.</note></term>—pitched their tents beside the waters (of Reason, but not beside the pure stream of the Spirit and of Life). And so, because of the dimness of their sight in such murky waters of human reason, they draw conclusions, glosses, and interpretations of Holy Scripture and "see the sun." Yet this gives them an entirely false and deceptive appearance, and presents the radiance of the eternal Sun—or the utterances of the living Word and Spirit of God in Holy Scripture—all upside down to their eyes. It is such that they would often swear an oath that the sun stood in those waters and cast its rays from there upward, even though it actually stands in the high heavens and shoots its rays down from there; and thus, that which one sees in the water is only a meager reflection of it.

Regarding this matter—and how far your mistakes and errors in this regard extend, since you view everything through your colored glasses (I mean through the rational conclusions, glosses, and compositions of your elders and ancestors) and want everything to be regulated accordingly, and whatever I will not regulate by them or allow to take on such a color is immediately condemned as heresy and damned—there would certainly be much more to say. However, that is not my purpose now, because my intention here is only to prove quite briefly, yet clearly and thoroughly, that the doctrine of the <term>Restoration of All Things<note>original: *Wiederbringung aller Dinge*; the theological concept that all fallen beings will eventually be reconciled with God.</note></term> is not contrary to Holy Scripture, but has its deep foundation therein, and is therefore no lie or false teaching, but a precious truth.

<summary>The author uses a metaphor of the sun's reflection in water to argue that theologians rely on distorted human reason rather than divine spirit to interpret the Bible. He claims his goal is to prove that universal restoration is a fundamental biblical truth despite the prejudices of the religious establishment.</summary>
<keywords>Reason vs. Spirit, spiritual blindness, Balaam, allegory of the sun, Restoration of All Things, heresy, biblical interpretation, religious authority</keywords>

But before I proceed to my **arguments of proof**, I will endeavor to clear out of the way a hard **stumbling block** in this doctrine, over which nearly everyone trips and takes offense. This stumbling block is namely that the witnesses of such truth are accused as if they believed and taught that not only all godless men, but even the **devils themselves** should be saved.

Against this, however, I hereby protest in the strongest terms in the name of all upright witnesses of such truth, and declare that such a thing is a false conclusion and a great untruth with which such witnesses are falsely charged. By this, people seek to make them appear truly black and hateful to the **blind masses** <note>original: *dem blinden Hauffen*—referring to the uneducated or spiritually undiscerning public.</note>. Neither the late **Lady Leade** <note>Jane Leade (1624–1704), an English mystic whose visions of the "Restoration of All Things" influenced many German Pietists.</note>, nor **Mr. Petersen** <note>Johann Wilhelm Petersen (1649–1727), a key figure in the radical Pietist movement.</note>, nor his **beloved wife** <note>Johanna Eleonora Petersen, a significant theologian in her own right.</note>, nor **Siegvolck** <note>Georg Paul Siegvolck, author of "The Everlasting Gospel," a seminal text on universalism.</note>, nor **Mr. Schäfer**, nor any other upright and sensible witness of such truth—the **Restoration of All Things**—has ever believed or taught this.

Nor do I believe that the devil—<note>NB: *nota bene*, Latin for "note well."</note> the **devil**, I say—will ever be converted and saved. However, I do believe this of those **angels** <note>The author distinguishes between the "spirit" of the angel created by God and the "diabolical essence" that later consumed it.</note> created so heartily and well by God the Lord, who, during their fall from God into sin, were entirely swallowed up by the devil and the diabolical essence. It is just as when some small leaves of fine gold are swallowed by **living mercury** <note>A common alchemical metaphor for the way one substance can absorb or hide another.</note> when one throws them upon it, or are perhaps hidden in a heap of ashes or earth. Or, to put it more precisely: Just as the **image of God** in man, through the **Fall into Sin**, was entirely swallowed up by the **Old Adam** and the shameful image of the serpent.

And because I now hope / that I have explained myself clearly enough regarding this point / and everyone will be able to understand very well / that the aforementioned accusation / is a fundamentally false imposition / and we do not believe at all / nor teach / that the Devil / or also the **old Adam** <term>old Adam: the fallen, sinful nature of humanity that must be "put off" according to Pauline theology.</term> / will ever be converted and saved / but instead hold / that these two godless scoundrels <note>original: *Vögel*, literally "birds," used here as a derogatory idiom for rogues or suspicious characters.</note> and monstrous freaks / as God’s sworn enemies / shall again die in all fallen angels and men / and be pulled out / or driven away from God’s good creatures / and be burned and consumed in the **wrath-fire** <term>wrath-fire: a term often used in Böhmean mysticism to describe the consuming, judgmental aspect of God's presence that destroys sin.</term> of God; but God’s good creature / which such evil enemies have swallowed up within themselves and held captive and dominated for so long in their wantonness / shall be raised up again from its fall and great misery and ruin / and be renewed to the glory <note>original: *Herzlichkeit*, here likely a variant of *Herrlichkeit* (glory/magnificence).</note> of the great GOD. This is now a serious and absolute command of Christ to all his disciples: namely, that they should go into all the world and preach the Gospel to **every creature** <term>every creature: from the Greek *pāse tē ktisei* (Mark 16:15). In this theological context, "creature" refers to everything created by God, including humans, animals, and spiritual beings.</term>; and that the holy Apostles of the Lord also faithfully fulfilled this command of their Master can be seen very well from their letters, and especially from the letter of Paul to the Colossians 1:23. In that place, he refers to it quite simply as a completely common and well-known matter when he writes: *Which you have heard, that it* (namely the Gospel, or the good news of the grace of God) *has been preached to every creature which is under heaven.*

Now see, dear sirs: If Christ the Lord commanded his disciples to preach the Gospel to every creature, and Paul testifies that they also faithfully fulfilled such a command and had preached the same to every creature under heaven, then truly! it must also concern every creature. <meta>This page continues the scriptural defense of universal reconciliation, moving from the logic of creation to specific citations from the Epistles of Paul and John. It reinforces the argument that if God created all things through Christ, He must also redeem all things through Him.</meta>

And just as it is now a precious, eternal, and indisputable truth that **GOD** the **Lord** created all things through **Christ**, so it also is and remains—according to the precious testimony of **Paul**—a precious and eternal truth that everything created by **GOD** through **Christ**, which subsequently fell into some enmity against him, shall be reconciled with **GOD** again through the same, even unto himself. This precious truth could then be proven and confirmed with many more testimonies from **Holy Scripture**, if it were necessary; for this very same Apostle writes in his letter to the **Romans**, chapter 11, verse 32, that **GOD** had consigned all under unbelief (and indeed for this purpose:) so that he might have mercy upon all. And in his letter to the **Ephesians**, he writes in chapter 1, verse 10, that **GOD**, according to the mystery of his will, intended to—or would—gather all things together again under one head in **Christ**. And **John** writes in his first letter, chapter 2, verse 2, that **Christ** is not only the reconciliation <note>original: *Versöhnung*; in this context, it refers to the theological concept of atonement or propitiation—the means by which the relationship between God and humanity is repaired.</note> for our sins, but also for the sins of the whole world—not to mention many other frequent testimonies.

And certainly, if even a single fallen angel or human being were to remain in an infinite enmity against **GOD**, or in sin (which is indeed the very enmity against **GOD**), then the Apostle could never have testified with any foundation that all things should be reconciled with **GOD** through **Christ**, and indeed unto himself. But because he has testified to it with very clear and express words, this testimony of his is true, and it will be fulfilled quite truthfully and certainly one day, whether it happens sooner or later.

<meta>This page continues the author’s response to a potential objection regarding the "eternal" nature of Christ's kingdom. ...surrender to God the Father and be subject thereunto, and thus would no longer be a King and Regent; then also quite indisputably those <note>The author uses the plural "eternities" to refer to the "aeons" or long ages of Christ's mediatorial reign.</note> eternities which were spoken of him as an eternal King and Ruler or Regent will end. However, it is well known to everyone that a King and a Kingdom are two entirely distinct things, and that one can very well come to an end while the other remains and continues. We have enough examples of this in the world at all times: namely, when a King ceases to be King and to rule over his kingdom—such as when he perhaps dies or through other circumstances—yet the kingdom remains in and of itself, it stands, and perhaps receives another King and Regent. In such a manner, Christ the Lord will then also, at the time when all things are subject to him and all his enemies lie as a <note>A reference to Psalm 110:1 and 1 Corinthians 15:25-28, central texts for the doctrine of the "Restoration of All Things."</note> footstool for his feet, cease to be King over his kingdom and hand it over to God and the Father. For it is a settled matter that the kingdom of Christ does not end when he hands it over to the Father; rather, he only ceases to be King and to rule over it. The Kingdom, however, remains under the Father’s government without end of time or days, and this does not contradict my words at all, as already mentioned.

<meta>This page continues the author’s argument from page 48, where he distinguished between the "office" of the King (which has a functional end) and the "Kingdom" itself (which is eternal). Here, he refines the definition of "infinity" as used by the prophets, suggesting it refers to the vastness of the kingdom's reach rather than just its duration in time.</meta>

I do not believe, however, that such words from the Prophet Daniel and the Angel Gabriel, concerning the infinity of the Kingdom of **Jesus Christ**, can or should be understood in such a way as if they were intended to refer to the infinity of time and days—even though the sense and meaning in that regard is also correct, and it will indeed remain without end. Rather, I believe instead that they pointed toward its mighty and indescribable, or boundless, greatness, and toward the innumerable multitude of its subjects, as is revealed quite clearly from other scriptural passages. 

For example, in **Psalm 72**, it is written of him and his Kingdom: He shall rule from one sea to the other, and from the river unto the ends of the world; those in the wilderness shall bow before him, and his enemies shall lick the dust; the kings of the sea and the islands shall bring presents; the kings of rich **Arabia** and **Sheba** shall bring gifts. All kings shall worship him, all nations <note>original: "Heyden," often translated as "heathens" or "Gentiles," referring here to all non-Jewish peoples.</note> shall serve him, etc. 

And one finds many more such scriptural passages which testify to the entirely boundless power and greatness of his Kingdom, and the innumerable multitude of his subjects. Thus, I believe that Daniel and Gabriel also pointed to this when they testified in the aforementioned words that there would be no end to the Kingdom of Christ. This is, moreover, quite common to the way the **Holy Spirit** speaks in Holy Scripture: namely, that He calls a matter "infinite" if it is in itself great and manifold, even if it is indeed contained within a certain number and limit. As for example:

<meta>This page continues the author's argument from page 49 regarding the use of the word "infinite" in the Bible. He moves from discussing the physical wealth of Israel to the spiritual "infinity" of Christ's kingdom, leading into a discussion on the ultimate fate of all created things.</meta>

...ple; **Isaiah 2:7** speaks of the Jewish people: Their land is full of silver and gold, and of their treasures there is no end; their land is full of horses, and of their wagons <note>original: "Wagen," referring to chariots or vehicles of war.</note> there is no end. In this same sense and understanding, it can also be said with the greatest justification that the **Kingdom of Christ** is infinite—namely, because of its power, greatness, and the diversity of its subjects. In my view, the words of **Daniel** and **Gabriel** should be understood with such meanings; and let this be enough on that matter.

However, you might ask me at this point: "Since you say here that eventually all the eternities mentioned in **Holy Scripture** will come to an end, do you then also believe that all creatures will then cease and pass away, or be reduced to nothing?"

He has prepared them for His infinite play of delight, love, and joy <note>original: "Lust- Liebes- und Freuden-Spiel." This mystical concept suggests that creation exists for God's holy enjoyment and harmonious interaction.</note>; indeed, only then will the dear God be truly loved, lauded, honored, and praised by all His creatures. At that time, He will have so fashioned all creatures that they, all together, shall praise Him in the most beautiful...

<meta>This page continues the author’s theological argument for the "restoration of all things." Having previously challenged the clergy's belief in the Devil’s eternal kingdom, the author now pivots to prove God’s intent, power, and wisdom using both Scripture and the natural world.</meta>

**Holy Scripture** testifies of Him that He is kind to all and has mercy on all His works (Psalm 145:9); that He loves everything that exists and hates nothing that He has made; that He spares all things because they are His, and His imperishable spirit is in all things (Wisdom 11:24-27, 12:1). <note>The author cites the "Book of Wisdom" (Sapientia), a book found in the Apocrypha, which was commonly included in German Bibles of this era.</note> Furthermore, it testifies that for this very purpose He confined all under unbelief, so that He might have mercy on all (Romans 11:32; Sirach 18:12); that He so loved the world that He gave His dear, only-begotten Son as its Savior, and sent Him into the world so that He might make it <term>blessed</term> <note>original: "seelig." But do you think He lacks the strength and power to carry out such an intention with all His heart? Indeed, when He speaks, it happens; and when He commands something, there it stands. And He Himself testifies of Himself that His <term>counsel</term> <note>original: "Anschlag." Refers to God’s sovereign plan or decree.</note> shall stand, and He will do all that He pleases (Isaiah 46:10).

...could be accused of a falsehood; but on the contrary, your groundless doctrine of eternal damnation <note>The author is challenging the traditional orthodox view that hell is unending, arguing instead for universal restoration.</note> brings the greatest shame and dishonor to our dear God; and you contend with all your propositions, with which you seek to maintain it, against all Divine attributes and virtues, such as His wisdom, omnipotence and justice, love, grace and mercy—all of which I would clearly and thoroughly convince you of, if I were not keen on being brief.

For with such a false doctrine of eternal damnation and hell-torment, and the unceasing power, dominion, and kingdom of the devil, you turn the almighty God into a completely powerless God, who could never overcome His enemies nor destroy their kingdom. You make Him into an ungracious, unkind, and unmerciful God, who, once He has cast His hatred and enmity upon someone, would never let them be reconciled or appeased again, even if He had avenged Himself a thousandfold upon them in His wrath. Indeed, you make Him into such a cruel tyrant that his equal has never been found among all the tyrants who have ever lived on earth; and into a completely unjust judge, who has placed infinite torment and entirely unspeakable hellish pain upon a temporal crime <note>A "temporal crime" refers to a sin committed within a finite human lifespan; the author argues it is logically and divinely unjust to punish a finite sin with infinite suffering.</note> and a few days or years of an evil life. and many more such ungodly conclusions are to be drawn from your doctrine, all of which run directly against God’s nature and virtue, as well as against His written word.

Yes / you say / because the ungodly sin against the infinite God, it is also right

For because **GOD** the Lord has created the creatures / and it was His will at their creation / that they should all be in an infinite blessed prosperity / and should laud and praise Him / so He will then also see to it / that such will of His shall yet again come to pass / and will raise up His fallen creatures again little by little out of their fall and make them blessed / through Christ; and will by no means leave them stuck in an infinite misery / because such is entirely against His nature—and also against His earnest will and purpose.

For behold / you dear sirs / I give you a very simple parable / and challenge all of you in such a parable upon your own heart and conscience / yes I ask you / answer me / whether you yourselves would be in a state / to desire such a cruel deed / as you attribute to the dear **GOD** with your doctrine of **eternal damnation**; for I set as an example: You had an enemy / who had also inflicted the greatest evil upon you / and such a one would then, because of his evil deed committed against you / be plagued with a great martyrdom and pain / in which he then before your eyes, like a poor worm / writhed / and out of great anguish and pain knew not / which way to turn / and for that reason day and night or constantly / with a piteous cry / implored you for grace and mercy / and thereby promised from the heart / that he would now love, honor, and serve you his whole life long / if you forgave him his debt and freed him from his great agony and distress / etc. Whether you would be in a state / to leave such a most-miserable and plagued human being / for only several days or weeks in such misery / without your heart breaking for him / and you having to take pity on him and be gracious to him / and forgive his debt. If you will now answer me hereupon / that you would not dare to do such a thing / that you could see him suffer before your eyes in his great misery for only a few weeks / and listen to his piteous, lamentable cry / because your heart would indeed immediately break / so that you would have to take pity on him / and be gracious to him; so I ask you further / why

<summary>The author argues for universal salvation, claiming that God's original intent for His creation's happiness must eventually prevail over the "eternal fire." He uses a moving parable of a suffering enemy to suggest that if humans are too compassionate to watch a fellow being suffer for a few weeks, God must surely be more compassionate than to allow never-ending torment.</summary>

    *   *Page 72:*
    *   Para 1: Compares God's mercy to a sea vs. human drop. Arguments against making God more unmerciful than humans.
    *   Para 2: Arguments against those who would leave enemies in misery. Human nature (even animals like a sow) helps those in distress. How much more God?
    *   Para 3: Conclusion on the "Restoration of All Things" (*Wiederbringung aller Dinge*). Calling to lay down swords.
    *   Para 4: Warning to those who persist in opposition. Reference to a "white Daniel" and the "number of years." Mention of elders/judges being ashamed.
    *   Para 5: Christ will defend His honor. The devil ("old devil dragon") will be defeated and forced to spit out his prey.
    *   Closing: Date and Location (Umstadt, 26 Sept 1724).
    *   Footer: ENDE (The End).
    *   Vocab: List provided in the source.

    *   *Para 1:* "Ihr dann den lieben GOtt weit unbarmherziger machet..." -> "You then make the dear God far more unmerciful than you yourselves are, though you have only a tiny droplet of His goodness..."
    *   *Para 2:* "Wolt ihr mir aber sagen..." -> "But if you would say to me, 'Yes, I would surely be able to leave my enemy in such misery,' then I say to you plainly that you are children of the devil..."
    *   *Para 2 (animal metaphor):* "wie man dann auch offt siehet/ daß wann nur eine Sau in einer Klemme steckt..." -> "as one often sees, that if even a sow is stuck in a trap and screams, the others run together and would gladly help her out..."
    *   *Para 3 (Apokatastasis):* "...der Wiederbringung aller Dinge..." -> "Restoration of All Things" (Universalism).
    *   *Para 4 (Daniel reference):* "einen weissen Daniel erwecken" -> "awaken a white [pure/wise] Daniel." Reference to apocalyptic calculations.
    *   *Para 5:* "Teufels Drachen" -> "Devil Dragon." "zerbersten" -> "burst asunder."
    *   *Closing:* Umstadt, Sept 26, 1724.

    *   *The "Sow" Note:* Explain the author is using a crude but relatable agrarian metaphor to illustrate basic empathy.<meta>This page concludes the author's argument for Universalism, or the "Restoration of All Things." Having previously argued that God's nature is fundamentally loving, he now challenges the reader to compare their own capacity for mercy with God's infinite compassion. The text ends with a bold prophetic warning to his critics and a final signature dated 1724.</meta>

You then make the dear **GOD** far more unmerciful than you yourselves are / though you have perhaps only a small droplet of His goodness, love, and mercy within you / while in Him there is a completely bottomless sea of it.

But if you would say to me / "Yes, I would surely be able to leave my enemy in such misery," / then I tell you plainly / that you are children of the devil / and not humans, much less Christians / and that you are liars in this matter / since I know for certain / that it would be impossible for you to do such a thing / because it is entirely against nature. For one often sees / that if even a sow <note>The author uses a blunt, agrarian metaphor here to show that even "lowly" animals possess a communal instinct for empathy that he believes some theologians lack.</note> is stuck in a trap and screams / the others run together / and would gladly help her out / if they were able. How much less / should one human leave another stuck in misery for so long / without having mercy on him / and helping him out / if he is able. From which parable you will right well be able to grasp and understand / how shamefully you treat the dear **GOD** / when you ascribe to Him such a tyrannical and hostile quality / which could much rather be reconciled <note>The author argues that human "justice" is often just a mask for cruelty, whereas God's justice is always restorative.</note> / although this parable does not yet by far reach the matter itself; for in the parable I speak of you as humans / and of your worst enemy / but in the matter itself I speak of **GOD** / as eternal love itself / and of His own creatures / who have their being through His will / and were created by Him.

And so I hope / that through this you are sufficiently convicted of the groundlessness of your teaching of infinite damnation <term>infinite damnation</term> <note>The orthodox belief that hell has no end, which the author is refuting.</note> / and will be moved / to lay down your spears and swords / in your heated struggle against the soul-saving doctrine and precious truth / of the **Restoration of all things** <term>Restoration of all things</term> <note>original: "Wiederbringung aller Dinge"; also known as Apokatastasis, the belief that all souls will eventually be reconciled to God.</note> / and to give **GOD** the glory; so / that you recognize and lament your previous blindness and folly in this matter / and laud and praise the infinite **GOD of Love** / that He is so good, merciful, and kind.

But if you will continue in your defiance and great contradiction / and continue to cry out "Unclean, unclean!" <note>A reference to the Levitical cry of a leper; here, he means his critics are labeling his "holy truth" as a spiritual contagion or heresy.</note> over such holy truth / then be surely assured / that such dealings will not succeed for you in the long run / for **GOD** the **LORD** Himself will take up His truth and honor / and awaken a **white Daniel** <note>In the biblical Book of Daniel and the story of Susanna, Daniel appears as a wise, young judge who exposes the corruption of the older, established judges. The "white Daniel" here refers to a future enlightened teacher who will prove the author's points using scripture.</note> / who understands the certain number of years / of which so much is written in the books / who will then investigate such matters quite thoroughly / and will reveal your great deception and outrage to all the people / so that you will then have to be ashamed of your folly before all the world / when it is found / that you / as the prominent judges and elders among the people / have erred so far in your judgment / and are yourselves guilty of that very thing or deed / of which you have accused others / and those / whom you have as it were condemned to death / are found to be righteous and innocent.

And even if no such physical Daniel should appear / yet I know / that **Christ** the **LORD** Himself / shall do this / and rescue His honor in this matter / and represent His truth / and not just one / but all of you / who dispute this truth / will run against it and be put to shame / but He will also bring an end to the **old devil dragon** / and throw [it] into his throat or gullet / so that he shall burst asunder / and must spit out again all the prey that he has ever swallowed / and thus make a total end to his kingdom and tyrannical lordship over the creatures. Yes, Amen! so let it happen / wishes from the whole heart / he / who wishes for the salvation of all men / and seeks only to please **GOD**.

->Umstadt, the 26th of Sept. 1724.<-

->T H E   E N D.<-

<summary>In this concluding section, the author uses a parable of animal empathy to argue that God's mercy must exceed human compassion, finally calling for the "Restoration of All Things." He warns his orthodox critics that they will eventually be put to shame by a "Daniel-like" figure or by Christ Himself, who will force the devil to release all "swallowed" souls.</summary>
<keywords>Restoration of All Things, Universalism, Daniel, Mercy of God, Umstadt, Devil Dragon, Salvation, 1724</keywords>
